# Navier–Stokes formalization: faithfulness audit

> **Machine-generated and unconfirmed.** Agents wrote every verdict and finding below; no human has checked them. A PDE expert must review this report before any public use.

- Paper: *Finite time blowup for Navier–Stokes* (OpenAI), 166 pages, sha256 `0e779481c4da40bd28d1e642e1d8ca57447d129610df28dfa5a11e9af8ae228f`, <https://cdn.openai.com/pdf/32d9f210-8b73-45e0-91bc-82a30aef8a9a/navier-stokes.pdf>, retrieved 2026-10-09.
- Lean: <https://github.com/openai/NavierStokesAndEuler> at commit `f9e8bc5b38b6e212696e8a30e3e91517af887bbd`.
- Method: agent audit: auditor, pairing and mathematics skeptics, judge. Each numbered item had one auditor and two skeptics (pairing, mathematics); a judge ruled wherever a skeptic dissented.
- Coverage: 80 of 80 items audited; 80 fully adjudicated, 80 judged.
- Review pass: a fresh checker tried to refute each of the 13 findings that mattered most (every medium one, and the one "absent" verdict): 0 refuted, 2 upheld as written, 11 reworded by a second adjudicator because a sentence claimed more than was checked, of which 6 went from medium to low. The 6 least confident OK verdicts were re-audited blind; 6 agreed. Findings the pass touched carry a Review line.
- Second audit: an independent re-audit on tend and agentbox (worker, mechanical gate, a reviewer who redoes the pairing and must try to refute) reached the first audit's verdict on 61 of 80 items. The 35 items on which the two differed in verdict, in a medium finding or in the stating declarations went to an adjudicator with both rulings and one rule: OK only if the paper's statement follows from stated Lean declarations by logic alone. Adjudicated: 35 (9 with the first audit's verdict, 10 with the second's, 16 where the verdicts already agreed).
- The rulings below are the second audit's where the two agreed, and the adjudicator's elsewhere.
- Generated 2026-10-10 from `audit-result.json`.

## Main theorems against Clay (C) and (D)

Three independent checks of `NavierStokes.Comparator.navier_stokes_breakdown_R3` and `…_periodic` against Fefferman's official problem statement.

### Definitions (`clay:definitions`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.88.

Yes. Both Lean theorems state (C) and (D) faithfully. For (D) this means the version with the official errata: the errata asks for a periodic pressure. I checked every clause in both directions: - **Data:** the Lean conditions on u₀ and f imply Clay's smooth/divergence-free requirement and (4), (5), (8), (9). - **Solutions:** every Clay solution, extended arbitrarily to t<0, is a Lean solution. No clause looks at values for t<0. So the Lean "no solution" claim is at least as strong as Clay's. What I confirmed directly: - (1), (2), (3) are encoded correctly, using Mathlib's Laplacian and gradient. - `ContDiffOn ℝ ∞` on univ ×ˢ Ici 0 means smooth up to t=0. ∞ is (⊤:ℕ∞), so this is C^∞ and not analytic. - (7) has an L² (`MemLp`) clause, so a non-integrable field cannot slip through with a Bochner integral of 0. - The pressure on ℝ³ is unconstrained, exactly as in Clay. - The class is not empty: v=p=f=0 is a valid solution. The remaining differences are conventional: - ν is quantified as ∀ν>0, with witnesses allowed to depend on ν. Rescaling makes this equivalent to "some ν > 0". - The multi-index bounds are written as Fréchet-norm bounds, with K real. - The time derivative is one-sided at t=0. - The periodic pressure condition is taken from the errata. ComparatorDefinitions.lean has the same definition text as the challenge file, and both are byte-identical to upstream formal-conjectures 8bf45ed apart from the namespace and notation.

- **low CONTENT**: Periodic solution class requires the pressure to be 1-periodic (from the errata). This is narrower than the main text of (D), so Lean (D) excludes only periodic-pressure solutions. It matches the official errata on page 6. Source: “The further condition p(x + ej , t) = p(x, t) should be made explicit in Eqn (8).” Consequence: Matches the errata-corrected (D), so the relation is equivalent. Against (D) without the errata it is lean_weaker. A solution whose pressure is q + c(t)·x corresponds to a periodic-pressure solution with the time-translated force f(x+Y(t),t), so such solutions are not excluded. The errata puts the condition in (8), a data condition; Lean treats it as a constraint on the unknown p, which is the only coherent reading.
- **low CONTENT**: ν is quantified universally: ∀ ν>0, ∃ (u₀,f), and the witnesses may depend on ν. This matches the natural reading of 'Take ν > 0'. It is equivalent to the 'some ν > 0' reading by parabolic scaling. Source: “Take ν > 0 and n = 3. Then there exist a smooth, divergence-free vector field u◦(x) on R3” Consequence: No defect; the Lean form is equivalent to or stronger than either reading. Under ũ(x,t)=c·u(x,ct), p̃=c²p(x,ct), f̃=c²f(x,ct), viscosity ν becomes cν and (4)-(11) are preserved. The repo uses the same rescaling (rescaledForce).
- **low CONTENT**: (4), (5), (9) bound each multi-index derivative ∂_x^α ∂_t^m. Lean instead bounds the operator norm of the total m-th Fréchet derivative, jointly in (x,t) with the max product norm, for every real K. The two are equivalent up to constants. Source: “\|∂xα u◦(x)\| ≤ CαK (1 + \|x\|)−K on Rn, for any α and K” Consequence: Different formulation, equivalent. \|∂^α_x ∂^k_t f\| = \|D^{\|α\|+k}f[(e_i,0)…,(0,1)…]\| ≤ ‖D^{\|α\|+k}f‖, and ‖D^N f‖ ≤ (n+1)^N · max over partials of order N. Real K, including negative values, adds nothing beyond K≥0 because 1+\|x\|+t ≥ 1.
- **low CONTENT**: Fields are typed on all of ℝ³×ℝ. The time derivative is the one-sided derivWithin on Ici 0, smoothness is ContDiffOn on univ ×ˢ Ici 0, and periodicity of f, v, p is required only for t≥0. Values at t<0 are unconstrained. Source: “p, u ∈ C∞(Rn × [0, ∞))” Consequence: Equivalent. No clause reads values at t<0, so restricting to t≥0 or extending arbitrarily gives a bijection with Clay's objects on ℝ³×[0,∞). One-sided differentiation at t=0 is the standard meaning of C^∞ up to the boundary, and univ ×ˢ Ici 0 is UniqueDiffOn.
- **low CONTENT**: Bounded energy (7) is encoded as MemLp (‖v·t‖) 2 for each t≥0, plus ∃E, ∀t≥0, a Bochner integral of ‖v x t‖² below E. The MemLp clause rules out the Bochner junk value 0 for non-integrable fields. Source: “\|u(x, t)\|2 dx < C for all t ≥ 0 (bounded energy)” Consequence: Equivalent. For a smooth u, a finite Lebesgue integral means L², and v(·,t) is continuous, hence measurable. With MemLp the Bochner and Lebesgue integrals agree, and C is uniform in t in both.

Second audit (`clay-definitions`): **confirmed**, confidence 0.86. Clause by clause the two theorems state Clay (C) and (D) faithfully. nu is universally quantified with hnu : nu > 0 and the data are chosen after it, matching "Take nu > 0 ... Then there exist"; n = 3 is EuclideanSpace R (Fin 3). The data classes reproduce (4), (5), (8), (9): u0 smooth and divergence-free (trace of the Jacobian), every derivative rapidly decaying; f smooth on R^3 x [0,inf) with the (1+\|x\|+t)^-K bound, or 1-periodic with the (1+t)^-K bound. The excluded solution classes reproduce (1), (2), (3), (6), (7) and (10), (11): the PDE for every t >= 0 with derivWithin on Ici 0, Mathlib's Laplacian and gradient (confirmed to be the componentwise spatial ones by the repository's own bridge lemmas), ContDiffOn smoothness up to t = 0, and one uniform energy bound. Three qualifications, all low: (D) additionally requires periodic pressure, which is the published Clay errata; the decay bounds use total iterated-derivative norms rather than multi-index partials; the L^2 clause makes Clay's (7) explicit. ComparatorDefinitions.lean repeats every definition line for line.

### Adaptation from formal-conjectures (`clay:upstream`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.87.

I diffed ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.lean against the upstream file it cites (formal-conjectures at 8bf45ed, fetched with WebFetch). Every definition and both theorem statements match upstream token for token. The only differences are the import, the inlined ℝ^n/ℝ³ notation (same right-hand side as upstream), the namespace, ∇⬝ becoming local notation, dropped category/AMS attributes, dropped (A)/(B), and docstrings. The fields queried on upstream main are unchanged, and main now marks (C)/(D) solved, citing OpenAI commit 8937a8f. That commit's comparator files are identical to the audited commit f9e8bc5. ComparatorDefinitions.lean and ComparatorSolution.lean repeat the same text. I checked the upstream encoding against Fefferman's p.1–2 conditions (1)–(11): ∀ν>0, the decay conditions (4)/(5)/(9), smoothness on ℝ³×[0,∞), the MemLp field plus the energy bound for (7), periodicity (8)/(10), and the one-sided time derivative at t=0. All are equivalent to the Clay text. The one real qualification is the official erratum. (D) requires periodic pressure, which is placed on the solution rather than in (8). This matches the amended Clay statement, and the paper's p.125 also cites it. But the unamended (D) is strictly stronger and is not what was proved. The proof genuinely uses pressure periodicity.

### Against the paper's claims (`clay:paper`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.85.

Yes. Both theorems state Clay (C) and (D) faithfully, (D) in the form fixed by the official erratum. The quantifier order is ∀ν>0 ∃(u₀,f) ¬∃(v,p): ν is fixed first and the data may depend on it. That matches "Take ν > 0 … Then there exist" in Clay, "For every ν > 0 there exist a force" in the paper, and "For every positive viscosity" in the README. Everything else is equivalent: (1)–(7) and (8)–(11), with C^∞ meaning ∞, not ω. Smoothness makes every derivative genuine, so junk values give no loophole. The solution file's statements match the reference text exactly. ComparatorDefinitions.lean matches the reference file line for line, minus the two theorems. The reference matches upstream formal-conjectures 8bf45ed, apart from namespace, notation, attributes and the removal of (A)/(B). I did not run Comparator, so this is not a kernel-level check. Remaining differences are all low severity: - (D) excludes only solutions whose pressure is periodic. This follows the erratum and is weaker than the unamended text. - The main theorems keep only the nonexistence sentence of Thm 1.1 and Cor 10.6. The blow-up construction is stated elsewhere, with project-internal definitions. - The README's torus bullet leaves out the periodicity requirement on the solution. - The decay and energy conditions are encoded by routine reformulations.

- **low CONTENT**: (D) excludes only solutions whose pressure is also 1-periodic, following the official erratum. Measured against the unamended text of (D) (velocity periodic, pressure only smooth), the Lean statement is weaker. Source: “The further condition p(x + ej, t) = p(x, t) should be made explicit in Eqn (8). (Clay errata, p.6)” Consequence: This matches the official statement, erratum included. The paper also relies on it: p.125 says 'as required in the erratum', and the uniqueness step on p.126 integrates the pressure term away by periodicity. Without the erratum, (D) would be a stronger claim that neither the paper nor the Lean proves.
- **low CONTENT**: The Comparator theorems keep only the nonexistence sentence of Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 10.6, quantified over the data. They drop the zero datum, the compactly supported force, the explicit blow-up solution on [0,1), its bounded energy and its L∞ blow-up. Relation: partial. Source: “For every ν > 0 there exist a force f ∈ C_c^∞(R³ × (0,∞); R³), a compact set K ⊂ R³, and smooth velocity and …” Consequence: Comparator's independent check covers (C) and (D) only. The paper's headline construction (abstract, Thm 1.1) is checked only against the project's own definitions. This does not affect the Clay claim.
- **low CONTENT**: The README's torus bullet says 'no global smooth solution exists'. The Lean excludes only solutions that are spatially periodic in velocity and pressure. The whole-space bullet leaves out the decay conditions (4)/(5), which the Lean does impose; that only makes the Lean stronger. Source: “for which there exist no solutions (p,u) of (1), (2), (3), (10), (11) on R³ × [0,∞). (Clay (D), p.2)” Consequence: Imprecise wording only. The README's appeal to '(D)' is accurate, because (D) restricts the competitors via (10) and the erratum.
- **low CONTENT**: Quantifier order is ∀ν>0 ∃(u₀,f): ν is fixed first and the data depend on it. This matches Clay, the paper and the README. It is stronger than an existential reading of 'Take ν > 0', and the two readings are equivalent by viscosity scaling. The data are not uniform in ν, which Clay does not ask for. Source: “Take ν > 0 and n = 3. Then there exist a smooth, divergence-free vector field u°(x) on R³ and a smooth f(x,t)” Consequence: Not a defect: the ∀ν form implies every reading of (C)/(D). The rescaling u^ν(x,t)=νu(x,νt), p^ν=ν²p(x,νt), f^ν=ν²f(x,νt) makes all ν equivalent anyway.
- **low CONTENT**: These encodings are routine reformulations of Clay's conditions: - (4)/(5)/(9) use the norm of the total-order Fréchet derivative, with a real exponent K, instead of mixed partials. - (7) adds MemLp at every time. - (1) uses derivWithin on [0,∞) and also holds at t=0. - (6)/(11) use ContDiffOn on univ ×ˢ Ici 0. All are equivalent for smooth fields. Smoothness also makes the solution class genuinely classical, so junk values give no loophole. Source: “\|∂_x^α ∂_t^m f(x,t)\| ≤ C_αmK (1 + \|x\| + t)^−K on R^n × [0,∞), for any α, m, K.” Consequence: different_formulation with no change of meaning: in finite dimensions, mixed partials and total-derivative norms bound each other up to constants. The statements are not weakened or strengthened.

Second audit (`clay-paper`): **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.78. Both theorems state Fefferman's breakdown alternatives with the quantifier order Clay asks for: `(nu : ℝ) (hnu : nu > 0)` is fixed first, the data `∃ u₀ f` after, so the data may depend on ν. Same order in paper Theorem 1.1 (p.1), Corollary 10.6 (p.125) and the README's "For every positive viscosity". (C) is faithful clause by clause: smooth divergence-free u₀ with (4)-decay, smooth f with (5)-decay, no pair (v,p) satisfying (1),(2),(3),(6),(7) on ℝ³×[0,∞). (D) is faithful to (8),(9),(10),(11) but also requires the hypothetical global solution's pressure to be 1-periodic. That clause is absent from the page-2 text of (D); it is what the Clay errata (p.6) adds, and the paper's own uniqueness argument (p.126) uses it. It makes the Lean (D) formally weaker than the unamended printed (D) — the single qualification behind `partly_confirmed`. ComparatorSolution.lean carries the reference names and signatures verbatim; its definitions come from ComparatorDefinitions.lean, which I compared line by line with the challenge file and found identical. Smoothness is `ContDiff ℝ ∞` / `ContDiffOn ℝ ∞`, not `ω`, in both, and `Space` is a reducible abbreviation of `EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)`, so no coercion intervenes.

### What the main theorems rest on (`clay-reachability`)

Second audit (`clay-reachability`): **confirmed**, confidence 0.82. NavierStokes.Comparator.navier_stokes_breakdown_R3 and ..._periodic (ComparatorSolution.lean:16,23) carry signatures identical to the reference statements Comparator checks (ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.lean:273,280), and those map clause for clause onto Clay (C) and (D): nu > 0 universally quantified, n = 3, smooth divergence-free u-naught, force conditions (4),(5) resp. (8),(9), and non-existence of (p,u) satisfying (1),(2),(3),(6),(7) resp. (1),(2),(3),(10),(11). Grep over NavierStokes/ found no sorry, axiom, native_decide, implemented_by, extern, unsafe or partial def; the four intentional sorries live only in ComparatorChallenges/, which no file imports. The chain ComparatorSolution -> ComparatorR3Theorem / ComparatorTheorem -> NavierStokesR3.theorem_1_1 (R3/Theorem.lean:46) / PeriodicPaper.periodic_corollary (PeriodicPaperTheorem.lean:155) reaches hypothesis-free theorems, and every lemma hypothesis along the way is discharged from the candidate or competitor structures; no undischarged hypothesis was found. The chain uses Theorem 1.1 in the paper's form, and Corollary 10.6 in an equivalent form re-encoded for 1-periodic lifts. Five low-severity notes follow; none bears on whether (C) and (D) are stated.

## The preprint's two claims

arXiv:2610.08144 (Bastounis, Circelli, Hansen) claims two mistranslations. Each was checked twice: once to verify it, once for its consequences.

### Claim 1, Lemma 8.6 (8.19): verification (`claim1:verification`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.90.

The claim checks out. (8.19) on p.95 says ‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m} ≤ C_m‖F‖_{C^{m+4}} for v_t=(√2−1,1), and its proof on p.96 uses \|v_t·k\|^{-1} ≤ C(1+\|k\|) plus a summable (1+\|k\|)^{-3} majorant. The Lean declarations exist at the cited lines and every one needs m+5: norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le (L1059), inverse_finiteJets (L865), both mixedJet_inverse_bound (PTI L654, SFTI L1082), and the derived realInverse_finiteJets and familyInverse_finiteJets(_fiber). The extra derivative comes from the lattice majorant Σ weight^{-4}, built as a product of two 1-D Σ(1+\|n\|)^{-2} sums. Σ weight^{-3} is also summable, so m+4 was within reach. I found no declaration that states the m+4 bound. Relation: lean_weaker by one derivative. The difference does not matter downstream. The paper only needs a fixed finite loss (p.64, p.96), and the class M_α (Def 6.4, p.69) is defined by bounds at every derivative order. Lean's MemClass.bounds is likewise ∀ m, and meanClass_temporalInverse absorbs the loss by calling hclass.bounds (m+5). The main theorems do not depend on the m+4 count. norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le and both mixedJet_inverse_bound are unused leaves. The bound that carries weight is inverse_finiteJets.

- **low CONTENT**: Every Lean torus-inverse estimate needs m+5 input derivatives where (8.19) needs m+4. No declaration states the m+4 version. The cause is the cruder lattice majorant Σ weight^{-4}. The difference is harmless because all downstream classes are all-order. Source: “‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_m ‖F‖_{C^{m+4}_y} (8.19); 'Four more derivatives of F than the requested output leave a summable (1+\|k\|)^{-3} bound'” Consequence: The paper's (8.19) is not formalized as written; Lean proves a version weaker by one derivative. The paper's later uses (p.96 'class estimates at every fixed derivative order retain their ε exponent'; p.64 'only a finite loss of torus derivatives') need only a fixed finite loss, and Lean's MeanClass/MemClass.bounds is ∀ m, so meanClass_temporalInverse(_moving) absorb the loss via hclass.bounds (m + 5). No effect on the main theorems.

Second audit (`claim1-verification`): **confirmed**, confidence 0.88. Every factual element of Claim 1 checks out. Lemma 8.6 on p.95 does state ‖N^{-1}F‖_{C_y^m} ≤ C_m‖F‖_{C_y^{m+4}} for N = v_t·∂_y, and TorusInverse.lean:197 defines .temporal = (√2−1, 1) = v_t. All four cited declarations exist at the cited lines with the cited `+ 5` pattern: norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le (SmoothFamilyTorusInverse.lean:1059–1080, hypotheses at `w.length + 5`), coefficient_seminorm_bound (SmoothFourierData.lean:368–387, `p + 4`), inverse_finiteJets (L865, `n + 5`), mixedJet_inverse_bound (ParametricTorusInverse.lean:654, SmoothFamilyTorusInverse.lean:1082). Every route to a torus-inverse estimate in the repository factors through coefficient_seminorm_bound (+4) composed with inverse_derivativeWord_bound (+1 from the divisor bound), so the loss is five everywhere; I found no declaration stating the m+4 bound. The clean downstream form, UniformFourierAlias.realInverse_finiteJets:611, is literally ‖N^{-1}f‖_{C^m} ≤ K‖f‖_{C^{m+5}}, which does not imply (8.19); "strictly weaker" is right. Going further: the extra derivative is a proof convenience (a product majorant Σ weight^{-4} in place of the paper's Σ(1+\|k\|)^{-3}, which converges on ℤ²), and both call sites absorb it — each draws hclass.bounds (m+5) from an all-order MemClass and returns the same α and the same slow power.

### Claim 1: consequences (`claim1:consequences`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.90.

The claim is accurate, but the gap it finds does no harm. Lemma 8.6, eq. (8.19) (p95) states ‖N⁻¹F‖_{C^m} ≤ C_m‖F‖_{C^{m+4}}. The paper's proof on p96 is sound: \|v_t·k\|⁻¹ ≲ 1+\|k\|, and Σ(1+\|k\|)⁻³ converges in 2D. The Lean declarations sit at the cited lines and need m+5. The extra derivative comes from the proof method. SmoothFourierData.coefficient_seminorm_bound uses the lattice majorant Σweight⁻⁴, the product of two 1D sums, instead of Σweight⁻³. No Lean declaration states the inverse with loss m+4. The bound is used through inverse_finiteJets and realInverse_finiteJets. These feed the temporal-update M_α class lemma (VariableGaugeMean.meanClass_temporalInverse_moving L2463), which sits on the main-theorem path, and the Lemma 8.2 alias bounds. Every consumer takes the class bounds at order m+5 (`hclass.bounds (m + 5)`), and MemClass carries bounds at every order. The paper also needs only some fixed finite loss: Definition 6.4 sets bounds at every multi-index. The m+5 version is therefore enough everywhere it is used, and neither main theorem is affected. The per-word lemma norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le and both mixedJet_inverse_bound declarations are not used anywhere, except that one feeds the other.

- **low CONTENT**: The torus-inverse estimate is proved with five lost derivatives, not four. Every Lean version (per word, full-jet, real, family) has m+5 where (8.19) has m+4. So (8.19) is not formalized as stated, only a version weaker by one derivative. Source: “‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_m ‖F‖_{C^{m+4}_y} (8.19), p95” Consequence: The Lean does not prove (8.19) as written, so the paper's sharper statement was not checked. The difference has no downstream effect. The paper uses only that the loss is finite: "only a finite loss of torus derivatives" (p64), and M_α holds at every order. Every Lean consumer reads the class bounds at order m+5. The main theorems are unaffected.
- **low CONTENT**: The same +5 loss carries into the bounds that rest on inverse_finiteJets. The radial alias of Lemma 8.2 is iterated with 5 torus derivatives plus 1 radial derivative per step, giving m+6p. Paper (8.10) states m+p+3. It is harmless for the same reason. Source: “‖L^{-p}H‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_{m,p}‖H‖_{C^{m+p+3}_y}, p ≥ 1. (8.10), p91” Consequence: This is outside Claim 1's cited declarations but has the same cause. Losses are absorbed by the all-order class bounds and alias decay, so the main results are unaffected.

Second audit (`claim1-consequences`): **confirmed**, confidence 0.82. The preprint's factual claim holds at every cited line. norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le (SmoothFamilyTorusInverse.lean:1059) bounds one torus derivative word of the inverse from sup bounds on f, on d_x^{\|w\|+5} f and on d_y^{\|w\|+5} f; coefficient_seminorm_bound (SmoothFourierData.lean:368) contributes p+4 and the divisor bound one more; inverse_finiteJets (:865) and both mixedJet_inverse_bound (:1082, ParametricTorusInverse.lean:654) repeat the +5. C^{m+4} control does not meet those hypotheses, so the Lean does not give (8.19) as displayed, and no declaration with a +4 torus-inverse loss was found. Consequences: none found. The extra order is an artifact of summable_weight_inv_four (SmoothFourierData.lean:343), which majorises weight^{-4} by a product; the repository never proves summability of weight^{-3}, which would restore m+4. Downstream the estimate is consumed only through inverse_finiteJets, by lemmas whose input is a class quantified over all derivative orders with an epsilon-exponent independent of the order (WeightedClasses.MemClass, PhysicalMeanDomain.LocalBandJets; the paper's Definition 6.4). The alias machinery even takes the loss as a parameter, instantiated at 5 (UniformFourierAlias.lean:852).

### Claim 2, (10.19) in Lemma 10.5: verification (`claim2:verification`)

Verdict: **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.88.

The declarations exist at the cited lines and say what the preprint reports. One correction: the R3StressPressureEstimate name is `regularized_flux_bound`, lowercase. No Lean declaration states (10.19) as the paper writes it. The Lean proves a different estimate by a different route, and neither inequality implies the other. Several of the preprint's listed differences are overstated. The commutator term R^{-7/4}B^{3/4} is exactly the paper's C_T R^{-1}·R^{-3/4}B^{3/4}, and it uses the same L^{4/3}/L^4 pairing and the same L^4 interpolation. The changes are confined to the local term: - L^{6/5}×L^6 instead of L^{3/2}×L^3; - Ḣ¹→L⁶ Sobolev plus L² Riesz bounds instead of L^{3/2} Calderón–Zygmund; - a φ² multiplier instead of φ⁴; - an A_R/R term and a 1/R² term. The sign difference (w = u−v) cancels inside the flux integrand. The Lean bound is sufficient for its only use. There, every power of A_R is at most 3/2, which is the property the paper's Young/Gronwall step needs. The Lean Lemma 10.5 (candidate_unique_on_Icc) has the paper's conclusion v = u on [0,T] and is fully proved with no sorry. So the claim that the statement is mistranslated is refuted for Lemma 10.5 itself. The main theorems are unaffected.

- **low CONTENT**: No declaration states (10.19) itself. The Lean bound contains A_R (A_R/R term), adds a 1/R² term and lacks the B_R^{3/2}/R and B_R^{1/2}/R shape. Neither bound implies the other. The commutator term is identical to the paper's. Proof-internal only. Source: “\|∫ π w·∇χ_R\| ≤ C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2} + R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}]. (10.19)” Consequence: Lean checked a different, valid estimate, not the paper's argument as written. Lemma 10.5 and the main theorems are unaffected, because the only consumer (exists_uniform_flux_absorption, which absorbs every term into δA²+D/R) is satisfied by the Lean form.

The result is R^{-7/4}B^{3/4}, the same as the paper's R^{-1}·R^{-3/4}.

It is a per-component bound for the Gaussian-regularized stress, rawBound with L² stress × L² flux factor. rawBound_le (:92) gives coefficient/R·(B^{3/2}+B+1). That is close to the paper's shape but not exact. Nothing imports this module, and it is not reachable from NavierStokes/ComparatorSolution.lean (import-graph traversal, 609 modules). NavierStokes.R3PressureFlux, R3EnergyAbsorption, R3EnergyNorms and R3PhysicalPressureBound are also unreachable.

It concludes ∀ t∈[0,T], ∀ x, u=v, and it holds for any CandidateProperties candidate, so it matches or generalizes the paper's lemma. Second audit (`claim2-verification`): **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.82. Every factual citation in Claim 2 checks out. The four named declarations exist at the cited lines (the name is `regularized_flux_bound`, not `regularized_flux_Bound`), and `exists_uniform_actual_pressure_flux_bound` (PressureFlux.lean:576) really proves CP[(B_R^{1/2}+1)(A_R/R+1/R^2)+R^{-7/4}B_R^{3/4}] for all t in (0,T), with A_R, B_R, chi_R = phi_R^8 defined as in the paper. No declaration states (10.19); the Lean inequality is incomparable to it even under (10.17), so the preprint is right that the displayed estimate was not transcribed, and right about the route differences (Hoelder 6/5-6 versus 3/2-3, cutoff split phi^2 * 8phi^5 grad phi versus phi^4 * 8phi^3 grad phi, \|\|u\|\|_3 versus \|\|u\|\|_6, L^6 Sobolev on the Riesz test versus L^3 interpolation of phi^3 w). Three qualifications: the commutator term R^{-7/4}B_R^{3/4} is identical to the paper's and is proved by the paper's own kernel route; the quantifier is strengthened from a.e. t to every interior t; and the Lean bound is exactly sufficient for the single use the paper makes of (10.19). The Lean's Lemma 10.5 conclusion (w = 0) matches the paper's.

### Claim 2: consequences (`claim2:consequences`)

Verdict: **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.88.

The cited declarations exist at the stated lines: PressureFlux.lean L516, L546 and L576. The fourth is named regularized_flux_bound (lowercase b), at R3StressPressureEstimate.lean L38. The L576 bound reads exactly as the preprint quotes it, and it does differ from (10.19). It has A_R/R and R^{-2} terms where the paper has R^{-1}(B+1)B^{1/2}. The two bounds are incomparable. The proof also takes another route: L^{6/5}×L^6 duality, Sobolev and L² Riesz bounds, a φ² commutator and ‖u‖₃. The paper uses L^{3/2} Calderón–Zygmund bounds, a φ⁴ commutator and ‖u‖₆. Some of the preprint's points are overstated. The commutator term R^{-7/4}B^{3/4} is the paper's R^{-1}·R^{-3/4}B^{3/4}, the same in both. The sign flips cancel, and both proofs interpolate. The Lean also holds for every t in (0,T), where the paper has a.e. t. No declaration states (10.19) in the paper's form. Its only use is in eq_of_pressure_flux_bound, through classical_uniqueness_on_Icc, and there exists_uniform_flux_absorption absorbs it into δA²+D/R, which is all the paper's Young/Gronwall step needs. candidate_unique_on_Icc concludes u=v on [0,T], matching Lemma 10.5. The main theorem is unaffected. The file R3StressPressureEstimate is an orphan module that no file imports. Calling this a "mistranslation" is an overstatement: it is a different, valid estimate used at an internal proof step.

- **medium CONTENT**: The Lean flux bound is not the paper's (10.19). Its local term (B^{1/2}+1)(A_R/R+R^{-2}) replaces R^{-1}(B+1)B^{1/2}. The two are incomparable, even after using B≲A+M/R from (10.17). The commutator terms R^{-7/4}B^{3/4} agree. Source: “\|∫ π w·∇χ_R\| ≤ C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2} + R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}] (10.19)” Consequence: The intermediate inequality checked is not the paper's. Both bounds are absorbable, though: every power of A is below 2 once B≤S(A+M/R). Lemma 10.5 and the main theorem are unaffected.
- **low CONTENT**: The proof route differs. Lean pairs φ²g in L^{6/5} with R_iR_j applied to the test function in L^6, using Sobolev and L² Riesz bounds. It commutes with φ² rather than φ⁴, and uses ‖u‖₃ rather than ‖u‖₆. The paper's L^{3/2} Calderón–Zygmund step is never formalized. Source: “We use the standard L^p boundedness of Riesz transforms for 1 < p < ∞” Consequence: The paper's argument for (10.19), as written, was not checked. A valid alternative argument was formalized and fully proved, with no sorry in these files.
- **low CONTENT**: The sign conventions are reversed: Lean takes w=u−v, π=p−q and tensorDiff=u_iu_j−v_iv_j=−g_ij, with u the reference solution. The flux integrand πw·∇χ_R is unchanged, so this is cosmetic. Source: “w = v − u, π = P − p, g_ij = v_iv_j − u_iu_j” Consequence: None.
- **low COVERAGE**: No declaration states (10.19) in the paper's form, (B+1)B^{1/2}/R + R^{-7/4}B^{3/4}. Only its ingredients exist: the R^{-3/4} kernel bound and the L^4 interpolation. The orphan R3Pressure* cluster uses yet another bound. Source: “Consequently \|∫ π w·∇χ_R\| ≤ C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2} + R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}]” Consequence: (10.19) is a display inside a proof, not a numbered item. Its role is filled by the Lean variant.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- Paper p.123 (img p-123.png): (10.19) holds for a.e. t, follows 'for almost every t ∈ (0,T)', and the paper factors out C_T R^{-1}. Paper p.122 defines A_R, B_R and χ_R=φ_R^8, (10.17) B_R ≤ C(A_R+R^{-1}‖w‖₂), and (10.18) commutes with φ_R^4. The commutator has L^{4/3} norm ≤ C_T R^{-3/4}.
- Paper p.121 Lemma 10.5: 'then v = u on that interval'. Lean NavierStokesR3.WholeSpaceUniqueness.candidate_unique_on_Icc (NavierStokes/R3/WholeSpaceUniqueness.lean:72) concludes ∀ t ∈ Icc 0 T, ∀ x, u(t,x)=v(t,x). Its hypotheses are smoothness on [0,T]×R³, UniformFiniteEnergy (Icc 0 T) v, div and NS on Ioo 0 T, zero datum and T<1. The statements match, and the Lean is slightly more general.
- Line numbers checked at commit f9e8bc5b: canonicalCutoffFlux_bound L516, exists_uniform_canonicalCutoffFlux_bound L546 and exists_uniform_actual_pressure_flux_bound L576 in NavierStokes/R3/PressureFlux.lean. regularized_flux_bound (lowercase) is at NavierStokes/R3StressPressureEstimate.lean:38. No declaration named regularized_flux_Bound exists.
- The only use of exists_uniform_actual_pressure_flux_bound is WholeSpaceUniqueness.lean:60 in classical_uniqueness_on_Icc. It feeds WholeSpaceComparisonClosure.eq_of_pressure_flux_bound (L32), which calls ComparisonRateBound.exists_uniform_rate_bound (L127) and exists_uniform_flux_absorption (L31): the envelope ≤ δA²+D/R given B ≤ S(A+M/R).
- Main-theorem chain: Comparator.navier_stokes_breakdown_R3 → ComparatorR3Theorem.lean:43 NavierStokesR3.theorem_1_1 → theorem_1_1_with_initial_rest (R3/Theorem.lean:26) → CandidateProperties.no_global_solution_one (CandidateBreakdown.lean:43) → candidate_global_agrees_before_one (WholeSpaceUniqueness.lean:104) → candidate_unique_on_Icc → classical_uniqueness_on_Icc. R3FiniteEnergyComparison.lean also calls classical_uniqueness_on_Icc.
- The import closure of NavierStokes.ComparatorSolution contains NavierStokes.R3.PressureFlux but not NavierStokes.R3StressPressureEstimate or the top-level R3Pressure* files. A grep shows no file imports R3StressPressureEstimate; it is built only through the lakefile glob.
- PressureRecovery.Hypotheses (NavierStokes/R3/PressureRecovery.lean:33) has no growth condition on the pressures. There is no sorry, admit or axiom in PressureFlux, WholeSpaceUniqueness, WholeSpaceComparisonClosure, ComparisonRateBound, LocalizedTensorBounds, PressureFluxTest, HeatKernelCommutator, RieszTestOperators or ActualPressureFlux (by grep only; nothing was built).

</details>

Second audit (`claim2-consequences`): **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.85. The cited declarations exist at the cited lines (regularized_flux_bound, not "_Bound", R3StressPressureEstimate.lean:38). Lean proves CP[(B_R^{1/2}+1)(A_R/R+1/R^2)+R^{-7/4}B_R^{3/4}] for every t in (0,T), not the paper's C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2}+R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}] for a.e. t; I found no declaration stating the paper's pure-B_R form, and neither inequality implies the other. The route differs too: L^{6/5}-L^6 duality with \|\|u\|\|_3 rather than L^{3/2}-L^3 with \|\|u\|\|_6, commutator multiplier phi_R^2 rather than phi_R^4, kernel-duality pairing rather than Young's convolution, w=u-v rather than v-u. Two sub-claims fail: the commutator term R^{-7/4}B_R^{3/4} is exactly the paper's, and I found no sign discrepancy (difference_energy_balance:115 has the p.123 signs). Consequences: none traced. The envelope is consumed only by eq_of_pressure_flux_bound, whose Young step absorbs it into (1/2)A_R^2+D/R exactly as p.123 does via (10.17), giving E'_R <= 2\|\|grad u\|\|_inf E_R + D/R, then Gronwall and R to infinity give u=v on [0,T] - the paper's Lemma 10.5 conclusion, which Lean states faithfully.

## Confirmed findings

### Medium (5)

<a id="f1"></a>**F1. Lemma 4.4: COVERAGE (medium)**, confidence 0.74

- The clause Delta T_0 = 0 is stated only for the axis-regular Profiles class. No leading stress is defined for the paper's class, pairs smooth merely on (0,infinity) x [-1,1], and the manuscript applies 4.4(i) to such a pair.
- Paper: “they apply on X > 0 even though this temporary inner branch need not be regular at the physical axis”
- Lean: LeadingStressWeights.stress (NavierStokes/LeadingStressWeights.lean:23) and its two components LeadingStress.theta, LeadingStress.axial (NavierStokes/LeadingStress.lean:34,38) are all under the section variable P : Profiles Omega (LeadingStress.lean:24); so are stress_eq (:26), stress_eq_of_coordinates (:84) and stress_outside (:98). ProfileHistories.Profiles (NavierStokes/ProfileHistories.lean:303) requires f ContDiffOn a RadialDomain, whose scale_mem (:34) forces the carrier to contain the axis point (0,eta), and E := sqrt(2X)*f. I listed all 78 LeadingStress / LeadingStressWeights declarations in decls.jsonl: none defines a stress for arbitrary fields. By contrast the other clauses of part (i) are axis-free: V_0 is SlowDivergence.radialFlux (:21) for arbitrary Field, a and b_s are ModulatedCone.angularShear / signedAxialShear (:24,:28) for arbitrary functions, Q_s, N_s and p_s are SeedHandbackJets.stocks (:90) for arbitrary Curves, and Delta m = 0 is NominalProfile.moments_propagate (:486) for arbitrary fields.
- Witness: Lemma 4.8(i) (p. 35) supplies the outer pair with U_o = 4 eta and E_o = P_* f(eta) (X/X_R)^{1/10} on (0,infinity) x [-1,1]. Then f = E_o/sqrt(2X) is proportional to X^{-2/5}, which is unbounded at X = 0, so this pair is not Profiles D for any RadialDomain D, and no Lean declaration assigns it a leading stress at all.
- Consequence: I traced the use. Lemma 4.8(i) (p. 35) fixes the definitions of V_0, Q_s, N_s and, through (4.11), the shear, stress and cone coordinates of that outer pair, and says these are the definitions used in Lemma 4.4. Page 40 then applies 4.4(i) to (U_0,E_0) against that outer pair and reads off that V_0, Q_s,0, N_s,0, p_0, s_0 and T_0,0 agree with the outer ones. The T_0 half of that conclusion lies outside the class any Lean stress declaration covers. Algebraically it would follow from (4.11) given Delta p_s = 0 and Delta a = Delta b_s = 0, all of which the repository does state axis-free, so what is missing is a stress object in that generality rather than an estimate; the clause is nonetheless stated nowhere. I did not trace a main-theorem dependence on it, so medium and not high.

<a id="f2"></a>**F2. Lemma 5.2: COVERAGE (medium)**, confidence 0.80

I traced the use: the manuscript cites (8.19) only inside Lemma 8.6's own proof (p. 96) and in the covering remark after (8.23), both of which need only 'the same fixed derivative loss in every chart'; and the Lean consumers quantify over all orders with a per-order constant (WeightedClasses.MemClass.bounds, WeightedClasses.lean:107), so meanClass_desiredIncrement (TemporalMeanUpdate.lean:1040) and the rest of the M_α chain are indifferent to 4 versus 5. Hence low: nothing downstream uses the paper's form.

<a id="f23"></a>**F23. Lemma B.1: COVERAGE (low)**, confidence 0.80

- Lemma B.1 defines J_ν as *the* smooth solution of YG_YY+νG_Y=F with G(0)=0 and asserts its coefficients are (B.5). Lean defines J_r by (B.5) and proves the converse direction only; no declaration states that these properties determine G.
- Paper: “the solution G of YG_YY + νG_Y = F that extends smoothly to Y = 0 and satisfies G(0) = 0”
- Lean: NavierStokes.AxisOperators.regularInverse, NavierStokes/AxisOperators.lean:470, defines J_r by the coefficient rule inverseScale, i.e. by (B.5). AxisEvaluationAlgebra.lean:478 (ODE), :148 (G(0)=0) and AxisEvaluation.lean:260 (smooth on strip I 20) prove that this element is a regular solution. Nothing states that a regular solution is this element.
- Witness: Recovering the paper's definite description needs an ODE fact the repository does not state: if H is smooth near 0, H(0)=0 and YH_YY+νH_Y=0, then (Y^ν H_Y)' = Y^{ν-1}(YH_YY+νH_Y)=0, so Y^ν H_Y ≡ 0 and H ≡ 0 — the homogeneous solutions Y^{1-ν} (ν≥2) and log Y (ν=1) are excluded by smoothness at Y=0. That is an analytic argument, not instantiation or unfolding of stated declarations.
- Consequence: The repository gives the existence half of the paper's characterisation, so a reader cannot conclude from it that the Lean J_r is the paper's J_ν by the paper's own definition. Downstream nothing can depend on the missing half, since it is stated nowhere; Proposition B.2 (p. 146) is an existence statement ('admits an analytic axis profile'), and AxisContraction.lean:711-726 consumes regularInverse only through the proved right-inverse direction. I did not trace the paper's proof of Proposition B.2.

<a id="f24"></a>**F24. Proposition B.2: COVERAGE (low)**, confidence 0.85

- The closing remark of the statement — that in unscaled radial derivatives the (B.13) bound reads C_{r,s} Lambda^{r-1} — was not found as a Lean statement; only the rescaled form and the r = 0 axial case are stated.

No declaration was found bounding any mixed derivative of the unscaled ProfileFamily.f / .U against the unscaled comparison profiles with a Lambda^{r-1} factor.
- Consequence: No mathematical gap. The clause is (B.13) with partial_X for partial_Y on the SAME scaled pair, so since Y = Lambda X each X-derivative contributes one factor Lambda and each eta-derivative none: pullback_partialY (NaturalProfile.lean:82-86) and pullback_second_Y (:91) state exactly this for r = 1 and r = 2, pullback_partialEta (:88) the eta half. Hence partial_X^r partial_eta^s of either scaled error = Lambda^r (partial_Y^r partial_eta^s)(Lambda X, eta) <= Lambda^r * jetBound eps R r s * profileErrorConstant d / (2 Lambda) = C_{r,s} Lambda^{r-1} with the same Lambda- and C-independent C_{r,s}. One chain rule from UniformMixedError, hence packaging rather than content.

- **Lemma A.6**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Clause (iii)'s ∂_z = 0 is proved for the swirl coefficient K/r, not for K itself: `heatCoefficient_partialZ` and `heatCoefficient_z_invariant` are stated for `heatCoefficient = physicalHeat C (1+h) p / √(2·p.2.1)`.
- **Proposition A.7**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No single declaration states the whole proposition: the moment/positivity/axis clauses are HeatedOutgoing.Specification, the stress-cone clause is HeatSwitchCone.preserves_true_cone. Both are about the same CompensationWitness, so they conjoin by taking the larger radius threshold.
- **Proposition A.7**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The paper lets chi_K be any smooth step that is 0 for y <= .2 and 1 for y >= .5; Lean fixes the single step sigma(log(X/switchRadius)/(3/10)). Lean's statement is an instance of the paper's family, not a quantification over …
- **Lemma A.8**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The axial half of (A.46) is written over s = r^2/2 (axialBackwardStress) rather than over r, so the two statements agree only after a change of variable.
- **Lemma A.9**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. Lean hypothesises smoothness of b on an open neighbourhood of K × [0,δ₀]; the paper writes smoothness on the closed rectangle itself. For a rectangle the two readings coincide, but Lean's is the formally stronger hypothesis.
- **Proposition A.10**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No single declaration states Proposition A.10. Its clauses are stated separately across TerminalHistoryBridge, TerminalCone, TerminalEdgeFactor, ActiveAnnulusWeight and TerminalEdgePaper; each clause was found and they conjoin routinely.
- **Proposition A.10**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The paper hypothesises Lemma A.8's regular axis and exact moments on a general field; the Lean theorems instead quantify over the constructed terminal profile and add SmallTail d and 32 \le switchRadius, discharging the moments inside the construction.
- **Proposition A.10**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The final clause of (A.51), that the constants do not depend on the physical scale q, is encoded structurally rather than asserted: the profile* family carries no q, and a separate lemma identifies the physical stress at the normalized parameter …
- **Lemma B.1**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No declaration states Lemma B.1, and none cites it. Its boundedness clauses are stated faithfully one at a time across about fifteen declarations in five files, so the pairing is inferred rather than declared.
- **Lemma B.1**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. The (B.6) variant with F replaced by A_X(F) has no declaration of its own, unlike the two derivative-omitted variants. It is the one-line composite of norm_inverseMixed_le with norm_average_le.
- **Lemma B.1**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. Lean's algebra constant is 64; the paper's proof route gives C_sq² = (8·π²/6)² ≈ 173. Lemma B.1 and (B.6) name no constant, so Lean's 64 and 5120/ε are its own sharper numbers, not the manuscript's.
- **Proposition B.2**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. Lean's unscaled angular field is g*Phi = phi/C, not the paper's phi = phi_* Phi. The amplitude C is divided out throughout, consistently with the pressure law partialY Pr = f^2 matching Pi_X = phi^2/C^2.
- **Proposition B.3**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Clause (v)'s radial-derivative half is not in Estimates: parameter_jets bounds only η-derivatives. Radial-order control exists separately (exists_mixed_jets for Φ and u; ProfileFamily.mixed_error), but not assembled for log Φ, p₁.
- **Lemma B.4**, CONTENT (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. The bundle ReferenceBoundsOnHold states p_{1,r} > 3 only at the single radius X = 100; the paper asserts it on the whole interval X_b ≤ X ≤ X_i. The interval version is a separate theorem in another file.
- **Lemma B.4**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The paper's unspecified cone margin c in v_r > 2 + c is instantiated in Lean as the literal 9/4, i.e. c = 1/4.
- **Lemma B.4**, CONTENT (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. The closing uniform-parameter-norm sentence is stated in a different file, under its own Λ-threshold hypothesis, and is never conjoined with the (B.23) bundle for one and the same Λ.
- **Proposition B.5**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No single declaration states Proposition B.5. Its clauses are distributed over exists_activation_continuation, Initial.activation_cone and natural_activation_direction (plus final_shears and the 'before X₀' equalities), all about the same witness.
- **Proposition B.5**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. The clause 'e_a vanishes to infinite order at X₀' is not itself asserted by any declaration found; the repository states the flatness of FlatCutoff.edge and the vanishing and smoothness of the activation, leaving the one-paragraph combination unstated.
- **Corollary B.6**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No declaration was found that bundles the corollary, or that binds t_c existentially with both t_c < t_1 and 4e^{t_c} < 4.1. The widths T, κ, δ stay ambient parameters of the regularity theorems.
- **Corollary B.6**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Clause (iv), that all subsequent modifications live strictly right of X_0 e^{t_c}, is stated only stage by stage: the transition ramp and the modulation each separately leave the collar alone. No declaration found quantifies over all later stages.
- **Proposition B.8**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No single declaration states Proposition B.8. Its clauses are stated separately: the exact match in NominalProfile, the relaxed cone in MatchingConeBounds plus RepairConeBounds, the ordering and existence in NominalConeAssembly, and the subsequent Q_s, N_s in NominalConeAssembly.Witness.
- **Proposition B.8**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The clause 'without changing its prescribed pressure datum' is definitional rather than proved in Lean: Controls.Pi is defined as F.axisDatum + C_p, so the constructed profile's datum is the reference profile's by construction.
- **Remark B.9**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No one declaration states the whole of (B.40). The chain is split: the outer data stage in exists_fixed_schedule, the (B_k) stage in ordered_seed_bounds, the rest in exists_ordered_prepared_continuation. Conjoining them is routine.
- **Remark B.9**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. The remark's closing clause — finite (not small) bounds at every other fixed order, radial cutoff derivatives possibly carrying inverse powers of the final widths — has no quantitative counterpart; only C^∞ smoothness of the cutoff was found.
- **Corollary B.10**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No single declaration bundles Corollary B.10. Its clauses are spread over exists_nominal_cone, the Certificate fields, seven Witness lemmas and the stress/analyticity lemmas, but they conjoin for the same W by logic alone.
- **Corollary B.10**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. The (B.40) order of choices is a property of the construction. It is visible only in the quantifier nesting of exists_ordered_prepared_continuation; the bare existential exists_nominal_cone cannot express it.
- **Corollary B.10**, CONTENT (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Controls records only kappa_le_one : kappa ≤ 1, while the paper fixes 0 < κ_0 < 1/2. At κ = 1 the activation factor (1−κ)σ(y/T) is identically zero, so the Lean existential does not guarantee a nontrivial flat factor.
- **Lemma C.1**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. (C.2) asks for a uniform margin in the four cone gaps Ψ of (4.35); the Lean asserts a uniform margin in a different, equivalent quadruple (a, v_s−2, P_c−2, U(P_c,J_c)−v_s). Equal positivity sets, different numbers.
- **Lemma C.1**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. (C.3)'s single collar width δ∂ with dist(X,∂I) < δ∂ is not in the family theorem, which gives an unquantified open N ⊇ B; the width comes from a second declaration in the same file.
- **Lemma C.1**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. The Lean hypothesis is ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ on an open O ⊇ K, i.e. data smooth past η = ±1; the paper assumes smoothness only on the closed rectangle I × [−1,1], with one-sided η-derivatives.
- **Proposition C.2**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No single declaration bundles clauses (i)-(viii). They are spread over exists_of_certificate, the Witness structure fields and five namespace lemmas, all for the same v, and conjoin by instantiation and routine algebra alone.
- **Proposition C.3**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. No declaration was found stating that the Proposition C.2 profiles satisfy all conclusions of Theorem 4.6. exists_weighted_profile bundles (iii)-(iv) only; the other clauses are separate lemmas, in six files, about the same witness.
- **Proposition C.3**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Clause (iii)'s three named lower bounds, and the single kappa in (0,2) of (4.26), are stated in those words by no declaration found. The repository does perform the compactness step for this witness and states its uniform consequences in its …
- **Proposition C.3**, CONTENT (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Clause (iii) asks the unit direction to extend smoothly to the closed annulus; the assembled closed-annulus statement gives only ContinuousOn. The smooth collar extensions exist but are not glued into a smooth closed-annulus direction.
- **Proposition C.3**, CONTENT (low): CONVENTION. Lean writes (4.26) additively in unnormalised tilt coordinates, epsilon <= 1+st and epsilon <= 2(1+st)^2-(v-2)(t-s)^2, where the paper writes a multiplicative margin 2-kappa on the unit direction. The same condition once the tilt is bounded.
- **Proposition C.3**, COVERAGE (low): ATTRIBUTION_GAP. Clause (i)'s η-analyticity is stated for these profiles, but in the repository's other encoding: holomorphy of the slow hierarchy's axis functions in the complex parameter, reached through the entrance scheme's identification of the base fields.

### Low (82)

- **(1.2)**, p. 2, section 1.1: absent. (1.2) is the incompressible Euler system, introducing the notation D_t u for the material acceleration. No declaration was found in the audited NavierStokes/ tree that states it: the whole tree never mentions Euler (a grep for `Euler` over NavierStokes/ returns zero hits, EuclideanSpace is spelled otherwise), and it has no named material-derivative operator ∂_t + (u·∇) — the `material` names it does carry (NavierStokes.PhaseCalculus.signedMaterialOp, PhaseCalculus.lean:139, and the PrimaryMaterialDefect/ActualPhaseDefect families) are phase-transport operators ∂_V + σε∂_T + b∂_R + (V/R)∂_θ + εG∂_Z in slot coordinates, not this system. The pieces of (1.2) are all present as separate definitions — temporalDerivative (∂_t u, ProblemStatement.lean:55), advection ((u·∇)u, line 63), pressureGradient (∇p, line 71), spatialDivergence (∇·u, line 67) — and the Lean states only the viscous equation (1.1): `navierStokesResidual ν u p t x = f (t,x)` with residual ∂_t u + (u·∇)u − ν•Δu + ∇p, imposed in CandidateProperties.navier_stokes (R3/ProblemStatement.lean:106) together with divergence_free (line 104). Substituting ν := 0 there would reproduce (1.2) verbatim, signs included (residual = f ⟺ D_t u = −∇p + f), but no declaration performs that substitution and every statement in the development carries ν > 0 (Theorem 1.1 quantifies over ν > 0); a grep for `navierStokesResidual 0` over NavierStokes/ returns nothing. The closest formula in the repository is Euler/EulerProof.lean:10738, `momentumResidual u p z = fderiv ℝ u z (1, u z) + gradient p`, whose first term is exactly D_t u (the spacetime derivative applied to (1, u)); it is the UNFORCED residual (no f), carries no incompressibility constraint, and lies in the Euler half of the repository, which is outside this audit's scope. Severity low: (1.2) is expository — it appears in §1.1 'Historical context and previous work', the notation D_t occurs nowhere else in the 166 pages (searched the text layer for 'Dt': two hits, both on this page), and no later step of the paper uses it. Lean: `NavierStokesR3.ProblemStatement.navierStokesResidual` (uses, NavierStokes/R3/ProblemStatement.lean:57); `NavierStokes.ProblemStatement.advection` (uses, NavierStokes/ProblemStatement.lean:63); `NavierStokes.ProblemStatement.spatialDivergence` (uses, NavierStokes/ProblemStatement.lean:67); `EulerLagrangian.momentumResidual` (uses, Euler/EulerProof.lean:10738).
- **display 8.2**, p. 8, section 3.1: different. The Lean's core is cut out by the same two similarity coordinates, but three things differ. (i) coreDomain is a space-time set over all t < 1, not the time slice C_τ; unfolding it at fixed t gives exactly a slice of C_τ's shape, so this one is pure packaging. (ii) The radial cap is the strict Λ·X ∈ (-20,20), i.e. 0 ≤ X < X_c with X_c = 20/Λ (X = s/q ≥ 0 automatically, s = r^2/2), where the paper closes the interval at X_c; open versus closed. (iii) The η cap is \|η\| < 11/10, which is no restriction at all: eta_sq_lt_one gives \|η\| < 1 at every point with τ > 0, so the Lean's "core" reaches the endpoints η → ±1, whereas the paper fixes η_c < 1 precisely to stay away from them — that is the hypothesis under which q ≍ τ, and hence under which the next two displays hold. In NaturalCore the enlarged set is used only as an open domain of smoothness and of the divergence identity (coreVelocity_contDiffOn, coreVelocity_divergence, exists_natural_core), where enlarging it is harmless; no declaration was found that imposes a cap η_c < 1 on a core region. Lean: `NavierStokes.NaturalCore.coreDomain` (defines, NavierStokes/NaturalCore.lean:43); `NavierStokes.NaturalCore.profileDomain` (defines, NavierStokes/NaturalCore.lean:40); `NavierStokes.NaturalProfile.domain` (defines, NavierStokes/NaturalProfile.lean:23); `NavierStokes.AxisEvaluation.strip` (defines, NavierStokes/AxisEvaluation.lean:156); `NavierStokes.NaturalAxisCoefficients.window` (defines, NavierStokes/NaturalAxisCoefficients.lean:27); `NavierStokes.SimilarityProfile.eta_sq_lt_one` (uses, NavierStokes/SimilarityProfile.lean:59).
- **display 8.3**, p. 8, section 3.1: different. The Lean fixes the two length scales as exact powers of q — radialLength q = q^{1/2}, axialLength q h = q^{1/2-h} — which is the paper's own later line on the same page (ℓ_r = q^{1/2}, ℓ_z = q^D, D = 1/2-h), not the comparability in τ displayed here. No declaration was found stating ℓ_r ≍ τ^{1/2}, ℓ_z ≍ τ^{1/2-h}, or the ratio ℓ_z/ℓ_r ≍ τ^{-h}; axialLength occurs in the whole repository only in Scaling.lean, in its definition and in core_axial_reynolds. The missing step is the passage from q to τ: tau_coordinate_identity gives τ = q(1-η²) exactly, so on \|η\| ≤ η_c one has (1-η_c²) q ≤ τ ≤ q, and raising this to the powers 1/2 and 1/2-h (both positive, since h < 1/100) gives the three displayed comparabilities with constants depending only on η_c and h. That composition is a few lines, but it is nowhere carried out, and the ratio ℓ_z/ℓ_r is never formed; core_radial_reynolds produces the same power q^{-h} from a different product (velocity × length), not from the ratio of lengths. - **display 8.4**, p. 8, section 3.1: different. Of the four clauses, only the azimuthal one has a counterpart, and it is pointwise rather than an L^∞ norm. Scaling.coreVelocity is only the nominal scale q^{-(1/2+h)} as a definition, with no field attached. No declaration was found stating any of: a supremum over a core region, a rate for ‖u_z^{(0)}‖ or ‖u_r^{(0)}‖, or the ratio bound O(τ^h) — the repository's expansion parameter q^{2h} (slowOrder h n = 2nh) encodes the same relative smallness structurally, but that is a different statement. What is not stated anywhere I found is the arrow: no declaration asserts that this azimuthal value diverges on such a circle, which would additionally need E(X_*,0) > 0, and no positivity statement for the constructed f (or for E = √(2X) f) was found. - **display 31.8**, p. 31, proof of Lemma 4.5: different. The two lower bounds are stated, the two upper bounds are not. Lean's `compact_normalized_cone` conclusion gives one constant ε > 0 with ε ≤ c x and ε ≤ 2 * c x ^ 2 - (v x - 2) * j x ^ 2 on K; with the paper's G := 2c² − (v−2)j² and ε = min(c_K, γ_K) that is exactly `c ≥ c_K` and `G ≥ γ_K` (merging two constants into one is a routine weakening in the direction the proof uses). The paper's upper bounds `v ≤ B_K`, `cv ≤ B_K` and the normalization `B_K ≥ 1` were not found as a statement or a proof step anywhere I searched: in their place UniformCone.lean:86 takes `B` to be an upper bound of the image under the explicit threshold function x ↦ max 0 (max (2/c x) (max (v x / c x) (4 * c x * v x / d x))), so the upper bounds on v and cv are never formed separately. Both devices are the same appeal to compactness and both deliver one threshold valid on all of K (`compact_large_lag`, SeedConePaper.lean:48, which is the paper's second assertion). The paper forms one closed expression in the three uniform constants, with two branches; Lean forms a pointwise four-branch maximum max 0 (max (2/c) (max (v/c) (4cv/d))) and then takes a finite upper bound B of its image on the compact K, setting p₀ = max 0 B (and P = p₀ + 1 in `compact_large_lag`). The branches correspond: the paper's (B_K+2)/c_K covers Lean's 2/c and v/c at once, since p ≥ (B_K+2)/c_K gives pc ≥ B_K + 2 > max{2, v} using v ≤ B_K and c ≥ c_K; the paper's 8B_K/γ_K dominates Lean's 4cv/d by a factor 2, since 8B_K/γ_K ≥ 8cv/G, the extra factor being what buys the margin γ_K/2 in eq-p032-1 rather than bare positivity. Lean's `max 0` branch has no counterpart in the paper's formula (the paper gets p > 0 from B_K ≥ 1). The surrounding assertion is still proved: `compact_large_lag` concludes the paper's statement, one P > 0 valid for every z ∈ K and every p₁ ≥ P. - **display 32.1**, p. 32, proof of Lemma 4.5: different. The identity half matches exactly; the quantitative lower bound does not appear. The paper's explicit threshold N ≥ 4C_Ψ C_rep/μ_R, stated in the sentence just above the display, is therefore not stated: the Lean threshold N is existential, the chain of two inequalities is not verified as written, and only its endpoint Ψ_i > 0 is. The Lean does have the margin notion, just not here: HasConeMargin ε p₁ p₂ A C (TrueConeLoop.lean:283) is literally 'all four cone components exceed their thresholds by at least ε', the Lean's analogue of the paper's μ_L/μ_R, and compact_periodic_trueCone_margins (TrueConeLoop.lean:289-298) upgrades pointwise strict cone membership on a compact slow set with a periodic fast angle to one uniform positive ε. Its only consumer is exists_compact_trueCone_family_with_margin (TrueConeLoop.lean:765-798, call at line 790), which applies it to the nominal loop family (A, C) over the slow parameters K; neither name occurs in ModulatedCone.lean, so the margin is never carried onto the repaired triple (a_f, b_f, p_f), and the repaired cone membership is re-derived from the stability radius instead. - **(5.8)**, p. 48, proof of Lemma 5.1: lean weaker. Same shape, strictly larger right-hand side. Term by term: p_k matches exactly (halfLength k = (k+1)/2 = ⌈k/2⌉); the Cauchy budget is split among the derivative letters only, as in the paper (word_radialBound, line 331); the radial/simplex factor is r^k/k! with r ≤ T = a, and the coefficient factor is M^k with M the row-sum bound on A₀ and A₁. Three differences. (i) The factorial is k!, not (k+1)!: the Lean counts only the k letters of the word and carries the final G inside the constant B bounding the input field F = G f_n (RadialBound … B 0), where the paper counts k+1 integrations and gets a^{k+1}/(k+1)!. (ii) The factor 2^k counting the compositions is left explicit instead of being absorbed into C_n^{k+1} as the paper does. (iii) The Cauchy factor is (max{1,Δ⁻¹} · max{1,p_k})^{p_k} rather than (max{1,p_k/Δ})^{p_k}; the two agree when Δ ≤ 1, and the Lean's is larger when Δ > 1. Each of (i)–(iii) is absorbable into a constant of the paper's own form C_n^{k+1} (since (k+1)·2^k ≤ 4^k), and none affects what the paper uses (5.8) for: summability is proved in summable_wordLayers (524) and used for convergence of the series. Also, the norm: the paper's S_{ρ'} = {η : dist(η,[−1,1]) < ρ'} is a neighborhood of the segment, whereas the Lean estimate is a sup over a closed parameter disk closedBall c ρ (with closedBall c σ inside the domain) and over the whole radial interval; applying it at each center c ∈ [−1,1] with σ = ρ_paper, ρ = ρ' covers S_{ρ'} with the same gap Δ. - **(5.13)**, p. 50, statement of Lemma 5.2: different. The weight, the edge distance and the domain match the paper exactly: `activeZeta c a b w = weight c a b (log X)` is the two-edge flat weight ζ(X); `activeDelta a b w = min 1 (min (log X - a) (b - log X))`, which with a = log X_a and b = log X_b is literally δ(X) = min{1, log(X/X_a), log(X_b/X)}; and `activeWindow a b = Ioo (exp a) (exp b) ×ˢ Icc (-1) 1` is {X_a < X < X_b, \|η\| ≤ 1}. `PolynomialEdgeJets` is then the paper's bound C ζ δ^{-N}, with two encoding choices in the Lean's favour: it bounds the whole derivative tensor ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ m f w‖ at each order m (its docstring says 'including mixed derivatives') rather than each ∂^I separately, so one constant serves all multi-indices of a given order; and the constants C, N are existentially quantified per order m, matching 'C_{n,I}, N_{n,I} may depend on the order and derivative'. What differs is the range of n. The Lean states this bound for the stress only at order n = 1 (`firstStress_edgeJets` on the whole active annulus, `modulated_first_edgeJets` on the outer collar for the concrete modulated coefficients). For n ≥ 2 no per-order weighted jet bound was found; the repository reaches the same place by another route: `HigherInteriorSupport` (the n ≥ 2 stresses are supported in a compact sub-annulus, see `modulated_higherInteriorSupport`) gives `higherStressQuotient_smooth_of_support`, i.e. 𝒯_n/ζ is globally C^∞ for n ≥ 2, and that smoothness — not a ζδ^{-N} jet bound per order — is what is fed into `normalizedTensor_weighted_bound`, together with `activeZeta_edgeJets` for the weight itself and `compact_weighted_jet_bound` on compacta, to produce the all-order conclusion `ConstructedSlowBase.WeightedStressBound`, which carries the paper's ζ(X)δ(X)^{-N} shape for the assembled cut tensor. So the formula as the paper writes it, with n ≥ 2, is not a Lean statement; what is stated instead is strictly the factorization 𝒯_n = ζ · (smooth) plus compact support, from which (5.13) for n ≥ 2 follows by Leibniz on ζ · (𝒯_n/ζ) with `activeZeta_edgeJets` and boundedness of the quotient's derivatives on the compact closure of the window. I did not carry that derivation out in Lean, and I did not verify the paper's closing remark that one may take N_{n,I} = 0 for n ≥ 2 against anything stated in the repository. What the repository states is the same route in pieces: the four X-coordinate coefficients (φ_n, U_n, β_n = V_n/X, Π_n, indices 0..3 of `extendedCoefficient`) are ContDiff on all of ℝ² and have tsupport inside Icc (-1) (s.B²/2) × the parameter window, and `compact_jet_bound` is exactly the paper's 'compactness of [0,X₊]×[-1,1] now yields the coefficient bounds', stated for an arbitrary smooth map on an arbitrary compact set. Instantiating it at F = extendedCoefficient s hI n i and K = Icc 0 (s.B²/2) ×ˢ Icc (-1) 1 gives, for each n ≥ 1, each i and each m, a constant bounding ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ m‖ on K, hence each mixed partial ∂_X^k ∂_η^ℓ with k+ℓ ≤ m (the partials are bounded by the derivative-tensor norm); off K the coefficients vanish, which is how the paper's unbounded range X ≥ 0 is reduced to a compact box. The fifth component F_n/X is `average` of the axial coefficient, smooth by `average_smooth`. So the difference is packaging: the instantiation to P_n with explicit constants C_{n,m} is not carried out in any declaration found, and Lean's bound is per-component in the operator norm rather than a max over mixed partials of the 5-tuple. - **(5.25)**, p. 55, statement of Proposition 5.3: lean weaker. The formula itself matches. Left-hand side: Lean bounds `navierStokesResidual (prefixVelocity N …) (prefixPressure N …) - prefixStressForce N …`. `navierStokesResidual u p = ∂_t u + (u·∇)u − Δu + ∇p` (ProblemStatement.lean:82), and `prefixStressForce = stressForce = tangentialStressForce`, whose Cartesian pack is (x₁/r·a, −x₀/r·a, −z) with a = (∂_r+2/r)T_θ and z = (∂_r+1/r)T_z; subtracting it therefore ADDS the paper's two stress-divergence terms, so the bounded object is exactly 𝓕_slow(𝒰^{[N]}) of (5.24). (I checked the sign on the axial slot, which is unambiguous: pack's third component is −z against e_z = (0,0,1); the θ slot is consistent with e_θ = (−x₁/r, x₀/r, 0).) The prefix fields are the uncut n = 0..N sums (`uncutPrefix h f J y = f 0 y.2 + ∑ j ∈ range (J+1), positiveCoefficient h f j y`), with the stress slot carried at base exponent −A−1/2, matching q^{−A−1/2+λ_n}𝒯_n. Exponent: Lean's rate 2Nh − 2 − M equals the paper's 2h(N+1) − K_M with K_M = 2h + 2 + M, which is independent of N; instantiating the theorem at M := m gives the paper's bound for each m with K_m = 2h+2+m, so the N-dependence and the N-independence of the loss agree exactly. Lean's constant C is existentially quantified after N and M are fixed, matching 'C_{N,m} may depend on N'. The norm is ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ m‖ (operator norm of the m-th space–time Fréchet derivative) where the paper uses max_{\|a\|+b≤m} \|∂_x^a ∂_t^b V\|; these differ by a dimensional constant absorbed in C. WHAT IS WEAKER: the paper asserts the bound at every point of the fixed compact profile range for every 0 < q ≤ 1; the Lean statement asserts it only `∀ᶠ z in l` for a filter carrying `PhysicalApproach l h lo hi`, which demands `Tendsto (fun z => (cartesianChart h z).1) l (𝓝 0)` — i.e. a germ statement valid for all sufficiently small q, on a compact carrier with t < 1, not a uniform bound over q ∈ (0,1]. Extending it to all q ∈ (0,1] would need a separate compactness/continuity argument that is not stated. The profile range itself matches: `innerBox lo hi = Icc lo hi ×ˢ Icc (-1) 1`, and the sole consumer `baseResidual_jetRate_axis` (BaseResidual.lean:2514) instantiates lo = 0, the paper's 0 ≤ X ≤ X_max, −1 ≤ η ≤ 1. Rated low because that consumer is the only use of `finite_error_rate` in the repository and it only needs the q → 0 form (it converts the estimate into `JetRate … m n` for every n ≥ 0, residual flatness), so the surrounding result is still proved. Separately, `FiniteIdentities h C d f` — the Step-1 exact cancellation identity — is a hypothesis of the Lean theorem rather than something it proves; that concerns the construction clause of Proposition 5.3, not inequality (5.25). Lean: `NavierStokes.BaseResidual.finite_error_rate` (states, NavierStokes/BaseResidual.lean:2477); `NavierStokes.DiagonalResidual.FiniteJetRate` (defines, NavierStokes/DiagonalResidual.lean:37); `NavierStokes.BaseResidual.prefixStressForce` (uses, NavierStokes/BaseResidual.lean:1182); `NavierStokes.SlowResidualMatching.tangentialStressForce` (uses, NavierStokes/SlowResidualMatching.lean:568); `NavierStokes.BaseResidual.PhysicalApproach` (uses, NavierStokes/BaseResidual.lean:350).
- **(5.26)**, p. 55, proof of Proposition 5.3: lean weaker. Same object and same shape of bound, but a cruder exponent and a non-explicit constant. (i) Exponent: the paper charges a/2 per transverse derivative, D = 1/2 - h per axial derivative and 1 per time derivative, giving q^{b - a/2 - Dk - l}; the Lean charges one power of q per physical derivative of any type, giving q^{b - m} with m the total order (the docstring at BaseResidual.lean:152-153 states this loss explicitly, and ActualBaseVelocityBounds.monomial_rate packages the same rate b - m on a filter). Since 0 < h < 1/2 gives 0 < D < 1/2, we have a/2 + Dk + l ≤ a + k + l = m, so for 0 < q ≤ 1 the Lean right-hand side is at least the paper's and is strictly larger whenever a or k is nonzero: the Lean bound is implied by (5.26) and not conversely. (ii) Constant: the paper's constant is explicit, C_m (1 + \|b\|)^m ‖g‖_{C^m}, linear in the C^m norm of the profile and polynomial in \|b\|; the Lean constant is a bare existential depending on f, b, the compact set and m, with no stated dependence. (iii) Domain: the Lean quantifies over a fixed compact set K with q ≤ 1 and X in Icc lo hi (comparable to the paper's fixed compact profile range 0 ≤ X ≤ X_max, 0 < q ≤ 1), and monomial_rate localizes further to a PhysicalApproach filter rather than giving a pointwise bound on the whole range. Severity is low rather than medium because the conclusion (5.25) that (5.26) is applied to allows a lump loss K_m independent of N and a constant C_{N,m} that may depend on N, so the crude loss m and the non-uniform constant still suffice: BaseResidual.finite_error_rate (BaseResidual.lean:2477) does prove a rate 2*N*h - 2 - M whose loss 2 + M is independent of N. Lean: `NavierStokes.BaseResidual.cartesianMonomial` (defines, NavierStokes/BaseResidual.lean:215); `NavierStokes.BaseResidual.cartesian_monomial_bound` (states, NavierStokes/BaseResidual.lean:250); `NavierStokes.ActualBaseVelocityBounds.monomial_rate` (states, NavierStokes/ActualBaseVelocityBounds.lean:345).
- **display 56.4**, p. 56, section 5.4: different. Three of the four entries match with the paper's exponents exactly: p_n with -2A + λ_n (basePressure), and the stress pair T = (T_θ, T_z) with -A - 1/2 + λ_n (baseStressTheta, baseStressAxial); A_n is streamFactor/summedPotential as in (5.27). The fourth differs in form: the Lean slow base has no separate entry B_n = u_{θ,n} e_θ. The azimuthal part is carried as the axial component K = swirlPotential (exponent 1/2 - A + λ_n on -C⁻¹∫_0^X φ_n) of the same vector potential, and is produced by the same curl; by velocity_zero/velocity_one the K-part of the curl is (-r ∂_s K) e_θ, an azimuthal field of the paper's shape, so the route differs but reaches an azimuthal field. The paper instead keeps B_n as a field and cuts it directly, relying on azimuthal fields with θ-independent amplitude being divergence-free (the Lean generic lemma, exists_realization, does provide that route through its B argument with hdivB/htan, but the slow base does not use it). Lean's divergence-freeness is then automatic, being a curl. Lean has no D-discount in the q-jet bound it assumes or proves: everywhere the loss is one power of q per derivative, i.e. the exponent 1 - m for the full space-time jet of order m, which is the D = 1 case of (5.28) — a value the paper's range 0 < D < 1 excludes. - **display 58.3**, p. 58, proof of Lemma 5.4: different. The paper writes the Faà di Bruno term structure of a mixed space-time derivative of χ(aq) with q = q(z,t): a prefactor a^k χ^{(k)}(aq) times a product of k derivatives of q whose multi-indices sum to (a',b'). No Lean declaration forms that expansion. What the repository states instead is (i) the exact one-variable chain rule `scaledCutoff_iteratedDeriv`, iteratedDeriv n (scaledCutoff a) q = a^n · χ^{(n)}(a q) — this is the paper's term in the degenerate case where q is itself the variable, so every ∂q factor is 1 and only k = n survives; (ii) the k = 1 case for a genuine composite χ(q(w.1,·)), as the `have hc` step at GenericSolenoidalRealization.lean:81, giving ∇(χ∘q) = χ'(q)·∇q; and (iii) for general order, the norm-level composition bound `composition_jet_bound` (a wrapper of Mathlib's norm_iteratedFDerivWithin_comp_le), ‖D^m(g∘f)‖ ≤ m!·C·D^m, which is what `cutoff_jet_bound` actually uses to reach the same conclusion without ever naming the individual chain-rule terms. There is also no z/t split: the Lean works with one total Fréchet order throughout. The surrounding result (the derivative bound of the next display) is proved, so the missing expansion costs nothing. - **display 58.4**, p. 58, proof of Lemma 5.4: different. Both statements bound derivatives of the composed cutoff by a negative power of q with a constant independent of the scale a, and the quantifier order matches (the constant is chosen before a, as the paper's 'with constants independent of a' requires). What differs is the exponent bookkeeping. The paper separates the orders: a' Cartesian space derivatives cost q^{-D} each and b' time derivatives cost q^{-1} each, with 0 < D < 1 coming from its hypothesis (5.28), \|∂_z^a ∂_t^b q\| ≤ C_{a,b} q^{1-aD-b}. The Lean has no D and no space/time split: `cutoff_jet_bound` takes a single total Fréchet order m, assumes ‖D^k q‖ ≤ B_k q^{1-k} (the D = 1 case of (5.28)), and concludes ‖D^m(χ∘(a·q))‖ ≤ C q^{-m}. Setting D = 1 and m = a' + b' makes the two coincide; for D < 1 the paper's bound is strictly sharper on q ≤ 1, since q^{-a'D-b'} ≤ q^{-a'-b'}. The Lean's hypothesis is correspondingly weaker (the paper's (5.28) implies it, because 1 - aD - b ≥ 1 - a - b and q ≤ 1), so the two implications are a consistent D = 1 specialization rather than a contradiction. The sharpening is a weaker intermediate bound that still suffices: the extra loss is absorbed into the per-order exponent reduction, the paper's ℓ'_m and the Lean's `CutStageEstimates.cutLoss`, and the downstream stage bound (`cut_product_bound`, `exists_cut_stage_constants`) is proved with it. Norms differ cosmetically too: the paper's \|·\|_m-style absolute value of a single mixed partial against the Lean's operator norm of the full iterated Fréchet derivative. - **(5.39)**, p. 59, proof of Lemma 5.4: different. No declaration was found stating this inequality: I found nothing bounding ‖D^m(F(U) − F(U^{[J]}))‖ by q^{-H_m}\|e\|_{m+s}(1+\|U^{[J]}\|_{m+s}+\|e\|_{m+s})^{d-1}, and no Lean object in this part of the development carries the paper's degree parameter d at all (Expression, GenericDifferentialPolynomial.lean:185-190, is an untyped syntax tree with no degree field). The Lean reaches the same place by another route: the differential polynomial is a syntax tree, and the comparison of F(U) with F(U^{[J]}) is propagated through it by structural induction (Expression.approximation_eval, line 217). At each product node ApproximationRates.mul performs the paper's own 'subtracting two monomials one factor at a time', splitting f·g − fs·gs = (f−fs)·g + fs·(g−gs) (lines 128 and 131) and adding the exponents: tailLoss m = max (maxJetLoss a.tailLoss m + maxJetLoss b.growthLoss m) (maxJetLoss b.tailLoss m + maxJetLoss a.growthLoss m). So the paper's factor (1 + \|U^{[J]}\|_{m+s} + \|e\|_{m+s})^{d-1} becomes an additive accumulation of the stage growth exponents over the remaining factors, the order shift m → m+s becomes ApproximationRates.directional bumping the jet index by one at each derivative node (line 162-164), and q^{-H_m} becomes a loss function of m alone, independent of J. The statement the paper derives from (5.39) and then uses — the difference has a J-independent loss, JetRate (f − stage J) m (gain J − loss m) — is what TailRates.rate records and what the rest of the Lean proof consumes; (5.39) itself, as a norm inequality in \|e\|_{m+s} and \|U^{[J]}\|_{m+s}, is not stated anywhere I found. The paper's display is a cover-shift identity at ONE fixed normalized band chart: 'fix the same normalized band chart', so (R,Z,T) is literally the same on both sides and the two sides differ only in the cover power J_g^{i_0(U)} against J_g^{i_0(V)}, with i_0(·) = min_{ℓ∈L(·)} i(ℓ) from (6.17) attached to the two neighborhoods U, V. The Lean has no instance of that form. Atlas.values_eq (ActualMeanPhysicalData.lean:116) is band-to-band: for two bands n, m valid at the same physical point z, Q_n^{-d} · f_n (A.chart n z) = Q_m^{-d} · f_m (A.chart m z). Since A.chart n = physicalToChartTZ h n (A.index n), which by physicalToChartTZ_apply (PhysicalMeanJetBounds.lean:40, rfl) sends z to (chartScale n · z.1, ((Q_n^{-1} z.2.1.1, Q_n^{-D h} z.2.1.2), coverMap (A.index n) z.2.2)), the two sides are evaluated at DIFFERENT points: each band's own rescaled (R,Z,T) as well as its own cover power. Setting degree = 0 removes only the Q^{-degree} prefactors; the arguments still differ (bandChart_chart:70-87 transports the radial component Q_n^{-1/2} z.1 to Q_m^{-1/2} z.1 through bandScale, and GaugeStateCoherence.bandChartEquiv_apply:361 shows bandChartEquiv carries bandScale and bandSlowEquiv alongside coverMap k). Within one band the index A.index n is fixed, so no two distinct cover powers ever meet at one chart. What the Lean checks in place of the display is the paper's following sentence, 'Both sides equal the original field in that chart': Atlas.physical_eq (:133) states A.physical U degree f z = Q_n^{-degree} · f_n (A.chart n z) for EVERY band n valid at z, so the physical field is independent of the representative; this is derived from the OverlapLaw hypothesis (:90), the band-overlap law with bandChartEquiv, not from the paper's fixed-chart cover shift. Two further encoding points: the Lean has a single open set U, not a pair U, V, the role of 'over U ∩ V' being played by the two hypotheses A.Valid U z n and A.Valid U z m; and Atlas.Valid (:96) is N ≤ n ∧ 0 < z.2.1.1 ∧ (A.chart n z).2.1 ∈ U, where z.2.1.1 is the first SLOW coordinate (the radius is z.1) — it equals 1 - w.1 under physicalPoint (PhysicalMeanJetBounds.lean:24), i.e. strictly before the terminal time, and CoherentFamily.field_eq (:314-318) discharges it with sub_pos.mpr hw for w ∈ PhysicalWaveSum.preterminal = {w \| w.1 < 1}. Severity low: the conclusion the display is used for — that the common-torus representation is well defined on overlaps — is proved, by Atlas.physical_eq and Atlas.family (:144), so the surrounding result stands; what is not checked is the paper's formula as written. No multi-index partial-derivative notation ∂^I was found anywhere in the NavierStokes namespace: the Lean measures regularity with the total Fréchet jet ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ j (f n) x‖ and quantifies over the order j ≤ m, not over a five-component multi-index. The chart's five real coordinates do exist (LocalSignedRequest.Point = ℝ × ((ℝ×ℝ) × (ℝ×ℝ)), with the slow pair (Z,T) and harmonic pair (H₁,H₂) read off by BaseContextAssembly.slowCoordinates), so the paper's I ∈ ℕ₀⁵ has a referent, but MemClass itself is stated for an abstract normed space D and never names R, Z, T, H₁, H₂. The two encodings are interchangeable in the direction the paper uses — ‖∂^I a‖ ≤ ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ \|I\| a‖ for every multi-index I of length \|I\|, since each coordinate partial is an evaluation of the \|I\|-linear iterated derivative on unit basis vectors — so the Lean bound is at least as strong as the paper's per-multi-index bound at the same order. I did not find a Lean lemma stating that comparison, and the converse direction (reconstituting the iterated-Fréchet norm from the coordinate partials) costs a dimensional constant; since this display only fixes notation for the bounds (6.24), (6.25) and (6.29), which the Lean states in its own notation, the surrounding results are still proved. Recorded as packaging/notation, not a mathematical gap.
