- **(6.31)**, p. 71, statement of Proposition 6.6: different. Both branches exist, but as class lemmas with no harmonic side condition and no cutoff hypothesis, and the harmonic bookkeeping sits in a different place. `wave_cmul_wave` gives W_α·W_β ⊂ W_{α+β} and `WaveClass.bilinear_mean` (scalar form `wave_cmul_mean`) gives W_α·W_β ⊂ M_{α+β}, each from 0 ≤ P ≤ 1 and ζ ≤ 1 — the first without 'm+m' ≠ 0', the second without 'm+m' = 0' and without any temporal-cutoff hypothesis, because Lean's `MeanClass` is the ζ-weighted derivative bound alone and contains no ∂_θ = 0 or support clause, so the M-branch is proved for every wave product rather than only for the zero-harmonic one. The clause 'with harmonic m+m'' is encoded instead by the grading: `field_mul` makes a product of harmonic fields the convolution product in AddMonoidAlgebra (α → ℂ) ℤ, so harmonics add, and `carrier_add` adds the phase coefficients. The genuinely zero-harmonic statement of the second branch is `angularProduct_mem`: the angular mean (zero harmonic) of a product of two finite harmonic fields with W_α and W_β coefficients lies in M_{α+β}. Exponents α+β agree in every case; the weight improvement from √ζ·P to ζ matches the paper's ζ^{3/2}P_v ≤ √ζ P_v and ζP_v² ≤ ζ on p. 72. - **display 71.5**, p. 71, statement of Proposition 6.6: different. No declaration stating the product for an arbitrary pair of derivative multi-indices I, J was found. Lean states the undifferentiated cross-label vanishing: `supported_cross_product_zero` gives u_l(x,θ)_i · v_k(x,θ)_j = 0 whenever label n l ≠ label n k (level ≥ 1), from disjoint slot supports, and `supported_product_germ` upgrades it to vanishing on a whole neighbourhood. Derivatives enter only where the fields are used: `supported_transport_zero` (via `transport_zero_of_product_germ`) kills cross-label advection, but the cylindrical transport operator differentiates the right factor only, so the case I ≠ 0 and J ≠ 0 simultaneously is never stated. The paper's form does follow from the germ form for smooth fields: {u ≠ 0} is open and v ≡ 0 there, so at a point x either a neighbourhood has u ≡ 0, whence ∂^I u(x) = 0, or x lies in the closure of an open set on which v ≡ 0, whence ∂^J v(x) = 0 by continuity of the derivatives; with the disjoint-tsupport hypothesis of `zero_germ_of_disjoint_on` this is a two-line argument. Rated low because the cross-label cancellation the paper draws from this line is what the Lean proves, by the germ route. No declaration forms the quotient r = z₋/z₊ or asserts that it is differentiable with this derivative; I searched the sources and the index and found no Riccati/quotient ODE anywhere (the only occurrences of this polynomial in NavierStokes/ are GrowingMode.lean lines 76 and 92). The Lean takes another route, which its own module docstring states: "The invariant region is proved using a quadratic boundary function, without dividing by the growing coordinate or assuming its positivity along the solution." In place of r it uses barrier t = (z t 1)² - r²(z t 0)² with r a *constant* half-width (a name collision with the paper's ratio function), and shows barrier ≤ 0 by a boundary-derivative argument. The two formulas are linked exactly: writing u = z₋/z₊, d/dt[z₋² - r²z₊²] = 2 z₊² u u' + 2 z₊ z₊'(u² - r²), so at u = ±r the barrier derivative is ±2 r z₊² times the display's right-hand side — and that right-hand side appears verbatim as the subject of upper_edge_factor_negative. The initial condition r(0) = 0 is stated, in the modal coordinates, by primary_initial (z₋(a) = 0 with z₊(a) = P(a) > 0). Only the paper's intermediate formula, not its conclusion, is missing: the cone bound it is used to prove is established (positive_invariant_cone, scaled_positive_invariant_cone). Severity low: (7.22) is explanatory in the paper (it is referred to only in the Figure 5 summary on p. 7, "Background shear amplifies the waves; viscous dissipation makes them decay", and in no later proof), and the conclusions it motivates are proved in Lean along the inequality route above. Lean's coefficient matrix for the same two blocks is the unfactored moment matrix `LocalizedMomentRepair.matrix a l u = fun i j => ∫ t, t ^ a i * bump (l j) (u j) t`. Its exponent rows are the paper's exactly (angularPowers λ = ![2, -2-2λ, -2λ]; axialPowers λ = ![1, 1-2λ]), but two things of the display are absent from it. (i) In the Lemma 8.7 pipeline the entries are never evaluated to μ_p e^{jdp}: the profiles there are bumps on pairwise disjoint cells of the patch rather than e^{jd}-dilates of one η_0, their moments stay as the integrals `FiveProfileMoments.bumpMoment` (:151), and nonsingularity comes from `PowerMomentMatrix.intervalMomentMatrix_det_ne_zero` (:216) rather than from the displayed factorisation. The factorisation is not missing from the repository, only from this matrix: for the two-row outgoing-pulse instance of the very same `LocalizedMomentRepair.matrix`, `OutgoingPulseBounds.actual_matrix_entry` (:565) evaluates the entries to μ_{p_i} e^{(p_i+1)c_j}, `normalizedMatrix_entry` (:582) puts row i in the displayed shape μ_{p_i}·(1, e^{d p_i}), and `normalizedMatrix_det_ne_zero` (:618) proves the determinant nonzero by μ_0μ_1(e^{2β_0} − e^{2β_1}) ≠ 0 — the paper's own argument of dividing the rows by μ_p and reading the Vandermonde in the distinct e^{dp}. That is a 2×2 instance with the pulse exponents −(1/2+λ), −(1/2+2λ), so it is not a counterpart of A_θ (3×3, powers 2, −2−2λ, −2λ) or A_z (2×2, powers 1, 1−2λ); the paper's method is formalised, for other rows. (ii) The row constants 1, 2a, -a and 1, a are not in the Lean matrix: they are divided into the target vector instead (angularDebt C d = ![0, -(d 0)/(2C), d 2/C], axialDebt C d = ![0, -(d 1)/C]), with the amplitude C of `background lam C R = C * R ^ (-1 - 2λ)` (FiveRowRank.lean:110) playing a(η) of (8.24). I checked these constants against the Lean rows themselves: pressure_row (line 189) gives 2C ∫ R^{-2-2λ}Δv = -d₀, angular_row (206) gives C ∫ R^{1-2λ}γ = -d₁, axial_row (223) gives -C ∫ R^{-2λ}Δv = -d₂, matching the 2a, a, -a of the display. Since A_θ = diag(1, 2a, -a)·M and A_z = diag(1, a)·M', the two parametrisations determine the same correction; what the repository does not contain is the explicit matrix as displayed. The nonsingularity the paper reads off the Vandermonde is proved for the Lean matrix at arbitrary distinct exponents (matrix_det_ne_zero), which covers the paper's exponents for λ > 0 (angularPowers_injective, FiveRowRank.lean:84). Hence low, not medium. - **(9.5)**, p. 104, statement of Proposition 9.3: different. No declaration was found stating the paper's packaged physical bound \|F^{[j]}\|_m ≤ C_{j,m,N} q^N for the stage's flat remainder. What the Lean states instead, by another formula, is all-powers flatness of each constituent of `errors.total` in chart scales. `NativeBounds N U gain loss f` unfolds (PhysicalResidualJetBounds.lean:375-380) to smoothness plus: ∀m ∃A ≥ 0, ∃p, ∀ l n, N ≤ n → ∀ x ∈ U, ∀ j ≤ m, ‖∂^j f_{l,n}(x)‖ ≤ A · Q_n^{gain − loss(m)} · S_n^p. The three constituents are bounded for EVERY real α, but not with identical data: `baseError_native` (:371) gives gain h·α, loss `fun _ => 0`, on the lifted domain; `axis_native` (:168) gives gain h·α, loss `fun _ => 0`, on the unlifted `strip.domain`; `gaussian_field_native` (:833) carries an extra band hypothesis hN and a per-order loss `fun m => 2·h·m`, on the lifted domain. So the gaussian chart bound already loses 2·h·m derivatives-worth of gain at order m: its exponent is h·α − 2·h·m, not h·α. All-powers flatness at each fixed order survives — α is universally quantified, so for fixed m the quantity h·α − 2·h·m is still arbitrarily large — but that step has to be said rather than assumed. Conjoining the three is therefore not available at loss 0: `excluded_nativeBounds` (PhysicalResidualJetBounds.lean:1027) requires one common N, U, gain and loss, and the repository supplies them in `native_residual` (:861-872), which first lifts `axis_native` to the lifted domain and weakens all three to gain h·(1/2+σ) with the common loss `fun m => 2·h·m` before calling it. Three differences from (9.5) as written. (i) Scale: the Lean bound is a chart bound in Q_n^{h·α−loss} with ε_n^α = Q_n^{h·α} (ChartScales.epsilon h n = Q n ^ h), not a physical bound in q^N; the repository's chart-to-physical dictionary is `selected_residual_jet_bound`, and it is applied to the realized state's full residual, not to the flat remainder alone, and it converts at a further derivative loss (physicalLoss h β m) that the paper's statement does not carry. (ii) Constant: the Lean's right-hand side keeps a slow factor S_n^p, so the constant is uniform over labels and points but not literally over bands; uniformity in the paper's sense is recovered by absorbing S_n^p into a slightly smaller exponent, which the ∀α quantifier permits but which I did not verify as a repository lemma. (iii) Range: the Lean bounds hold for bands n ≥ 4, and on the lifted strip domain for the base error and the gaussian field but on the unlifted strip domain for the alias, where the paper states them outright and on one domain. Severity low rather than medium because the surrounding argument is still proved: the Lean's downstream use of these three bounds is exactly `native_residual`, which deliberately weakens all the flat terms to the single common gain h·(1/2+σ) and common loss 2·h·m, so the later argument never needs (9.5)'s arbitrary power. - **display 112.1**, p. 112, proof of Lemma 9.7: different. No declaration defines a function D_{v,a}(m) indexed by a source vertex a, and nothing in the repository builds a DAG of the correction construction. The same bookkeeping is carried out twice, each time inside one inductive expression type rather than once for the whole construction. (i) GenericDifferentialPolynomial.Expression has nodes input / coeff / add / mul / directional — the paper's "sums, products, derivatives" — but no node for the "proved inverses" the paper also lists as graph operations, and no node of general weight d_{v,w}: a derivative node adds exactly one order, so an operation costing d derivatives is d stacked nodes. Its ApproximationRates.threshold : ℕ → ℕ propagates by the paper's recursion: directional gives threshold m = a.threshold (m+1) (the edge weight d_{v,w} = 1), add gives max of the two children at the same m (d_{v,w} = 0), a coeff node gives 0, matching the initial value D_{b,a}(m) = 0 for b ≠ a, and in polynomial_jetRate_of_stages the input nodes are given threshold = id, matching D_{a,a}(m) = m. mul takes sup over k ≤ m of each child's threshold rather than the child's value at m; every threshold built here (id, 0, max, m ↦ θ(m+1), and sup over k ≤ m) is monotone, so that sup equals the child's value at m and the two agree, the Lean version being the safe choice for a non-monotone threshold. Two things do differ from the display. The propagated quantity is literally the approximation-stage index J required for the tail estimate at jet order m (tail_rate : ∀ m J, threshold m ≤ J → …), not a count of derivatives of an input field; the two are identified only through the hypothesis htail (m ≤ J) of polynomial_jetRate_of_stages, which is what forces threshold = id at the sources. And one function is carried instead of one per source a: the maximum over sources is taken as the recursion proceeds, so what is computed is max_a D_{v,a}(m), the coarsening the paper's conclusion uses. (ii) FlatKernelBounds.Expr.jetOrder is the same recursion for the kernel expressions: k at a jet k source node, 0 at every non-source node, max over the children at add and mul; there the m + d_{v,w} shift is performed by rewriting the expression with Expr.diff, which sends jet k to jet (k+1), instead of by shifting the counting function's argument. Neither construction is applied to the correction-stage graph of Lemma 9.7. In that chain the finiteness of the derivative counts is not needed as a separate step, because every stage field is asserted smooth of infinite order (ActualCandidateAssembly.stages_smooth : ∀ j, ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (potentialStages …) (physicalDomain …), and likewise for directStages and pressureStages, lines 879-889) and the per-order estimates are stated with the derivative order m quantified in front of the constant (WholeDomainStageBounds.actual_finite_residual_bound). So the paper's formula as written was not checked; what was checked in its place is the same recursion for two restricted classes of expressions, plus infinite-order smoothness of the stage fields. Row 3 (time, 1+h) is `native_time_power_le` verbatim. Row 2 is `CoordinateAlgebra.D h = 1/2 - h`, and it is used exactly as the axial entry `Q ^ (-D h)` of `chartLinear`. - **display 113.6**, p. 113, proof of Lemma 9.8: lean weaker. The Lean bound charges (1+h) for every Cartesian derivative of the physical phase, spatial ones included: for total order i = a+b it gives Q^(-i(1+h)) = Q^(-(a+b)(1+h)), where the paper gives Q^(-a/2 - b(1+h)). Since Q ≤ 1 and h ≥ 0, -(a+b)(1+h) ≤ -a/2 - b(1+h) with equality only when a = 0, so the Lean factor is the larger loss whenever a ≥ 1: the Lean statement is implied by the paper's, not conversely. The hypothesis a+b ≥ 1 matches (`1 ≤ i`), the slow factor S_*^{P_{a,b}} matches (`S n ^ ((d+1)*i)`, a power allowed to grow with the order), the constant depends only on the order and on fixed data, and the geometric-in-i form of the Lean bound is a strengthening in shape, not in the Q power. The loss is not recovered elsewhere: the same uniform (1+h) is what `common_jets` proves for the graph composition and what every consumer uses. - **display 113.8**, p. 113, proof of Lemma 9.8: lean weaker. The oscillatory factor matches exactly: `character ((carrier h n)*(j)) Φ = exp(i·k·m·Φ)` with `carrier` the rounded integer frequency k and j the harmonic m, and the constant is allowed to depend on the order and on a bound H for \|j\|, as the paper's C_{a,b,m} does. The Q exponent differs: the Lean charges `derivativeCost h = s_t = 1 + 3h/2` for each of the m Cartesian derivatives whatever their direction, giving Q^(-m s_t) in place of the paper's Q^(-a s_x - b s_t) with m = a+b. Since s_x = 1/2 + h/2 < s_t = 1 + 3h/2 for h ≥ 0 and Q ≤ 1, the Lean factor is the larger loss whenever a ≥ 1, so the Lean bound follows from the paper's and not conversely. The slow factor S_*^{P_{a,b,m}} matches (`S n ^ (d+e*m)`). Severity is low because the paper itself collapses to the worst case one paragraph later: its ℓ_m = 2A+(m+1)s_t (eq-p114-1) charges s_t for every derivative, and that is exactly what the Lean's `physicalLoss` reaches. The Lean fixes the radii absolutely instead of choosing them: r_0 = z_0 = 1/4 (supportCylinder is r² ≤ 1/16, \|z\| ≤ 1/4), and the plateau on which the cutoff is 1 is r² < 1/32, \|z\| < 1/8 — the axial plateau is exactly z_0/2 but the radial plateau r < 1/(4√2) ≈ 0.177 is larger than the paper's r_0/2 = 1/8, so the Lean plateau is not the paper's. Most importantly the paper's choice is constrained: it picks τ_0, z_0 with C_0(τ_0 + z_0^{1/D}) < q_*/2 so that by (10.3) the support of c lies in q < q_*/2, inside the local domain Ω_* = {τ > 0, q < q_*}; no such constraint, and no occurrence of q_* or of (10.3), appears in SpatialLocalization.lean. - **display 118.3**, p. 118, proof of Proposition 10.1: different. No τ_0 makes the Lean switch an instance of the paper's χ_t: timeSwitch vanishes on \|t\| ≤ 3/8, i.e. for 1 - t ≥ 5/8, which needs τ_0 ≥ 5/8, while it is known to equal 1 only for t ≥ 3/4, i.e. 1 - t ≤ 1/4, which needs τ_0/2 ≥ 1/4, so τ_0 ≤ 1/2 — and the paper additionally demands τ_0 < 1/2. The Lean switch is also only shown to vanish on a two-sided neighbourhood of 0 (\|t\| ≤ 3/8) rather than on all of 1 - t ≥ τ_0, i.e. nothing here rules out t < -3/8. The product c(x,t) = χ_x(x)χ_t(t) is never formed as a single function in Lean: the two cutoffs are applied in sequence (TimeLocalization.activatedVelocity applied to the spatially cut field), which is the same product pointwise. Rated low because both consequences the paper's proof draws from χ_t are stated for the Lean switch — the fields vanish near t = 0 (used for the initial-rest clause) and the switch is 1 on the late interval where the paper's and Lean's fields must agree (t ∈ [3/4, 1), as in PaperLocalization.local_theorem_with_compact_candidate) — and the numeric gap between 5/8 and 1/2 is a different admissible choice of constants, not a weaker statement, since the Lean never needs the support of c to sit inside the paper's Ω_*. - **display 118.7**, p. 118, section 10: absent. No declaration was found that states this Poisson equation with a force-divergence term on the right. The closest asset, and a structural analogue of this very display, is `NavierStokes.R3WeakPressure.momentumGradient_divergence` (NavierStokes/R3WeakPressure.lean:120-126): with `momentumGradient U V G i = Δ(U i) - ∂_t (U i) + V i - ∑_j ∂_j (G i j)` (same file, :88-91), V occupying the force slot and G the velocity stress, it concludes `divergence (momentumGradient U V G) = -(∑_i ∑_j ∂_i ∂_j (G i j))` — the paper's identity with `div f` dropped, because it assumes `hV : divergence V = 0` (:124). That hypothesis is exactly what the paper's following sentence flags as possibly failing ("the force may have nonzero divergence"). In the downstream statements I opened, the force slot is filled by the literal zero function: `R3DifferenceStress.momentum_equation` (NavierStokes/R3DifferenceStress.lean:110) and `R3LocalizedPressure.localized_pressure_gradient` (NavierStokes/R3LocalizedPressure.lean:126) both write `functionMomentum U (fun _ _ => 0) G`, and `smooth_pressure_gradient` (NavierStokes/R3SmoothPressure.lean:124-142) carries the same `dV : functionDivergence V = 0` through to `pressure_gradient_unique` (:161), which is the only consumer of `momentumGradient_divergence` that I found. The force-free pressure identity itself is `R3PressureFourier.stressPressure_poisson` (NavierStokes/R3PressureFourier.lean:363): `Δ (stressPressure g) = -∑_i ∑_j ∂_i ∂_j (g i j)`, again with no force term. `NavierStokesR3.ConservativeDifference.weak_pressure_poisson` (NavierStokes/R3/ConservativeDifference.lean:431) is a third, further-off relative: a weak identity tested against ψ ∈ C_c^∞ rather than the pointwise display, written for the difference w = u - v of two fields whose residuals are equal, so the two forces cancel and no `- div f` appears; it belongs to the comparison argument of Lemma 10.5 (paper p.122, display Δπ_* = -∑ ∂_i ∂_j g_ij), not to the §10.1 localization. So the picture is not that the repository never writes a pressure Poisson equation, but that every version of it I found assumes the force (or the difference of forces) is divergence free, which is the one feature the p.118 display exists to record. My searches (listed in `searched`) over the index and over the `NavierStokes/` tree found no declaration taking the spatial divergence of `navierStokesResidual`, and no identity retaining a divergence-of-the-force term; I did not read all 815 files. The paper's own derivation is three lines from (10.5) plus div u = 0 (div ∂_t u = 0, div Δu = Δ div u = 0, div((u·∇)u) = ∑ ∂_i ∂_j (u_i u_j), div ∇p = Δp), but that derivation is not carried out in Lean for these fields. Severity low: the display is terminal in the paper — it carries no equation number, and "Poisson" occurs exactly once in the whole text layer, in the sentence immediately after it — and I found no later step of §10 or of the Lean development that uses it, so Proposition 10.1 and Lemma 10.2 are unaffected. Lean: `NavierStokes.R3WeakPressure.momentumGradient_divergence` (uses, NavierStokes/R3WeakPressure.lean:120); `NavierStokes.R3PressureFourier.stressPressure_poisson` (uses, NavierStokes/R3PressureFourier.lean:363); `NavierStokesR3.ConservativeDifference.weak_pressure_poisson` (uses, NavierStokes/R3/ConservativeDifference.lean:431); `NavierStokes.R3WeakPressure.pressure_gradient_unique` (uses, NavierStokes/R3WeakPressure.lean:161).

The Lean theorem's statement (GenericEndpointExtension.lean:455-458) is only `UniformContinuousOn (iteratedFDeriv ℝ n f) s` — it carries no constant, no increment and no (n+1)-st derivative. The quantitative inequality lives one level down, as the proof step `hLip : LipschitzOnWith K (iteratedFDeriv ℝ n f) s` at line 461, whose constant `K = ⟨max C 0, _⟩` (line 460) is internal to the proof: it is a Lipschitz bound on the whole joint jet over an open convex s, for any C bounding ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ (n+1) f‖ on all of s, with the displacement measured in the full (t,x) norm rather than \|t'-t\|. Specialising s to a product neighbourhood and the two points to (t',x) and (t,x) gives a bound of the paper's left side, but with the larger right side \|t'-t\|·sup_s‖(n+1)-jet‖ in place of \|t'-t\|·sup_{K_0×[t,t']}\|∂_x^α∂_t^{j+1}G\|, so the displayed inequality as written is not supplied. The use the paper makes of it — every fixed derivative is uniformly Cauchy as t ↑ 1, so the limits exist — is proved: `actualJet_uniformContinuousOn` feeds `exists_limit_of_uniformContinuousOn` (GenericEndpointExtension.lean:481,488) and then `OffplaneJetExtensions.extension_of_local_jets`, whose docstring records that the endpoint derivative limits are constructed there rather than assumed. The paper's requirement b_j > b_{j-1} is then imposed separately by scale = DiagonalScale.doublingEnvelope localScale, which is strictly monotone and in addition at least doubles at each step (2 * scale j ≤ scale (j+1)) — a condition the paper does not ask for. The displayed multilinear (Andreief-type) expansion of det B as an m-fold integral over I_1×⋯×I_m of det[x_j^{α_i}] ∏_j β_j(x_j) is not in the Lean sources; neither is the paper's sign claim that det[x_j^{α_i}] has constant sign on the ordered set {0 < x_1 < ⋯ < x_m} (only its nonvanishing is stated, at powerEvaluationMatrix_det_ne_zero, PowerMomentMatrix.lean:143). The Lean reaches the display's conclusion det B ≠ 0 by a different route: bumpMomentMatrix_det_ne_zero (line 261) rewrites B as intervalMomentMatrix against the measures volume.withDensity β_j and invokes intervalMomentMatrix_det_ne_zero (line 216), which argues by vecMul-injectivity — a column combination c with Bᵗc = 0 makes ∫ powerSum a c dμ_j = 0 for each j, so an averaging/intermediate-value lemma (exists_zero_of_setIntegral_eq_zero, line 158) yields a zero x_j ∈ I_j, the x_j are strictly ordered by interval separation, and powerEvaluationMatrix_det_ne_zero plus the Rolle induction expSum_coefficients_zero (line 74) force c = 0. So the paper's formula as written was not checked; the statement it is used to establish (invertibility of B, Lemma A.1) is stated and proved. Matrix orientation matches the paper: Lean's entry i j is ∫ t^{a i} β j t, i.e. B_{ij} = ∫ x^{α_i} β_j(x) dx. Lean: `NavierStokes.PowerMomentMatrix.bumpMomentMatrix` (defines, NavierStokes/PowerMomentMatrix.lean:252); `NavierStokes.PowerMomentMatrix.bumpMomentMatrix_det_ne_zero` (states, NavierStokes/PowerMomentMatrix.lean:261); `NavierStokes.PowerMomentMatrix.powerEvaluationMatrix_det_ne_zero` (implies, NavierStokes/PowerMomentMatrix.lean:143).
- **display 141.4**, p. 141, proof of Lemma A.8: different. The right-hand equality ∫_0^∞ (U^2 - E^2/2) dX = 0 is stated verbatim: `totalS`/`energyDensity` are exactly that integrand and integral over `Ioi 0`, and `energy_zero` asserts it vanishes. The left-hand equality — the physical radial moment ∫_0^∞ r (u_z^2 + p) dr and its conversion to the similarity integral by the factor q^{1-2A} — was not found as a declaration or as a step: searching the index and the Lemma A.8 chain turned up no integral of a physical pressure or axial velocity over the physical radius in this file group. Lean instead keeps the q-powers pointwise (`physicalPressure_eq_terminal` gives p = q^{-2A}Π, and the same bridge carries u_z = q^{-A}U) and obtains the vanishing it needs for the stress limits from `HeatTailHistoryLimits.energyHistory_tendsto_zero` (S(∞) = 0) together with X·Π → 0, used in `TerminalHistoryBridge.axialWeight_tendsto_zero`. So the same conclusion is reached by a route that never forms the physical moment; the prefactor q^{1-2A} as written is not checked anywhere I found. The surrounding result is still proved, hence low. - **display 141.5**, p. 141, proof of Lemma A.8: different. The displayed chain is the Fubini exchange that converts the tail pressure identity into a radial moment: with p(r) = -∫_r^∞ u_θ(r')^2 dr'/r', swapping the order of the two integrals gives the inner ∫_0^{r'} r dr = r'^2/2 and hence -½∫_0^∞ r' u_θ(r')^2 dr'. The same algebraic identity (radial pressure moment = -½ times the next moment of the pressure derivative) is stated by `IntegratedMeanBalances.pressure_moment`, but over all of ℝ for a compactly supported smooth p inside the Section 8 mean-balance machinery; I found no use of it, or of any Fubini exchange, in the Lemma A.8 / terminal-stress chain. There Lean never forms ∫_0^∞ r p(r) dr at all: `TerminalStress.canonicalPressure` and `HeatedOutgoing.Pi` take the tail integral as the definition of the pressure and the argument proceeds pointwise in the similarity variable, so the exchange step as written is not reproduced. Searches for a nested `∫ x in Ioi 0, ∫ …` and for `Fubini` in these files returned nothing (Fubini appears only in the R3/Fourier files). Low: the identity is elementary and the surrounding result is reached without it. Lean: `NavierStokes.IntegratedMeanBalances.pressure_moment` (states, NavierStokes/IntegratedMeanBalances.lean:146); `NavierStokes.TerminalStress.canonicalPressure` (defines, NavierStokes/TerminalStress.lean:456).
- **display 155.1**, p. 155, section B: different. The Lean reaches the same conclusion (S_q > 1 through the transition) by an exact identity plus a compactness absorption, not by the paper's explicit error expansion; neither displayed inequality appears in the Lean with the paper's right-hand side. (i) First line: instead of -H_cℓ_i′ ≥ LΛχ - Cχ - C_{σ_*}√χ - C/Λ - o(1), the Lean has the exact identity H·realGradient = -L·χ (gradient_identity), so the leading term is ΛLχ with no error at all, and the whole discrepancy between the actual old gradient and Λ·realGradient is carried as a single additive term g with \|g\| ≤ B (actual_shape_source_threshold hypotheses, ReferenceJetBounds.lean:153, ActivationContinuation.lean:349). The paper's separate Cχ, C_{σ_*}√χ, C/Λ and o(1) terms have no counterpart: a grep of NavierStokes/ for Real.sqrt applied to chi returns nothing, and the 1/Λ dependence enters only as the evaluation point t = 1/Λ of shapeRemainder, absorbed qualitatively by shapeModel_uniform_lower (MatchingConeBounds.lean:129) via compact absorption and a small-perturbation argument rather than by a named constant. (ii) Second line: the equality -H_c(log f)′ = 2ηH_c/(1+η²) is exact in Lean (logShape_hasDerivAt together with shapeGradient). The lower bound is not -Cj_0² - C/Λ - o(1) but the explicit constant -1/500, and it is proved for H_* = H h j η (the axis data), not for the actual H_c: the Lean does not complete the square, it uses η·shapeGradient η = 2η²/(1+η²) ≥ 0 and bounds the cross term by \|d·j·shapeGradient\| ≤ 2j ≤ 1/500 from j ≤ 1/1000, which is linear in j rather than quadratic, hence a weaker but sufficient intermediate bound. The 1/Λ correction to H_c (shapeRemainder's Hc = H + t·d·v 1, line 85) is again handled by the compactness perturbation. The surrounding result is nevertheless proved: actual_shape_source (MatchingConeBounds.lean:732) gives 1 < 5/4 < sourceQ on the transition, and actual_shape_relaxed (line 776) carries it through to the relaxed cone. Lean: `NavierStokes.MatchingConeBounds.logShape_hasDerivAt` (states, NavierStokes/MatchingConeBounds.lean:22); `NavierStokes.MatchingConeBounds.shape_axis_lower` (contains, NavierStokes/MatchingConeBounds.lean:66); `NavierStokes.MatchingConeBounds.shape_axis_lower` (contains, NavierStokes/MatchingConeBounds.lean:71); `NavierStokes.NaturalProfile.gradient_identity` (states, NavierStokes/NaturalProfile.lean:284); `NavierStokes.MatchingConeBounds.shapeSource_identity` (states, NavierStokes/MatchingConeBounds.lean:166); `NavierStokes.MatchingConeBounds.actual_shape_source_threshold` (states, NavierStokes/MatchingConeBounds.lean:197); `NavierStokes.OutgoingEntranceCone.shapeGradient` (defines, NavierStokes/OutgoingEntranceCone.lean:161); `NavierStokes.NaturalAxisData.chi` (defines, NavierStokes/NaturalAxisData.lean:38).
- **(B.40)**, p. 157, statement of Remark B.9: different. (B.40) is not an equation but a dependency chain: a summary of the admissible order in which the parameters may be chosen (each entry may depend only on entries to its left). The Lean does not display this chain anywhere; it encodes the ordering structurally, as the nesting of ∃/∀ in several separate theorems, each covering one segment. Segment-by-segment, from the declarations read in source: (i) M_d, P_*, λ, h: `exists_ordered_profile_cone` fixes m (= M_d) first, then P ≥ amplitudeThreshold m (= P_*), then lam0 (λ), then h ≤ heightThreshold m lam (so h after M_d, P_*, λ), with X_R last (∃ XR0, ∀ XR > XR0); T_d is not a separate Lean choice because the paper fixes T_d = e^{M_d} + 10 from M_d. (ii) tolerance → j_0 → δ_*, σ_*, Λ → T_sh → C: `exists_ordered_matching_threshold` puts ∃ eps (the matching tolerance) before ∀ j, then ∀ prep : AxisPreparation F j (whose fields `delta`, `sigma` are δ_*, σ_*), then ∃ Λ0 ∀ Λ ≥ Λ0, then ∃ T (= T_sh) before ∃ C0 ∀ C ≥ C0; `exists_ordered_prepared_continuation` repeats the same nesting with ∃ rho, ∃ jcap before ∀ j and ends with ∀ J ∀ epsilon ∃ tolerance ∃ w, i.e. the finite control order and tolerance last. (iii) (B_k) → C → widths: `ordered_seed_bounds` produces B, K, BJ from Λ before ∀ C and quantifies the ramp parameters T κ w₁ w₂ after C, which is the paper's statement (p. 157 line above (B.40) and p. 156) that B_k may depend on Λ but is independent of large C, and that the widths come last. (iv) the C → X_R link: `eventually_matching_geometry` gives radiusFloor < matchingRadius F C = 110·(C·P)^10 for large C, i.e. X_R is determined by C, matching X_R = 110(C P_*)^10 in the paper. What I did not find: a single declaration stating the whole chain, and any Lean parameter identifiable as the collar shear factor κ_0 or the first-collar width t_1 of (B.26); the κ, w₁, w₂ of `ordered_seed_bounds` are the activation-ramp damping constant and ramp widths, which I did not verify are the paper's κ_0, t_1, ω_fin. The surrounding results (Remark B.9 and Corollary B.10) are about this bookkeeping only, and the ordering they need is realized in the quantifier nesting of the theorems above, so this is packaging rather than a mathematical gap. Lean: `NavierStokes.MatchingDebtBounds.exists_ordered_matching_threshold` (states, NavierStokes/MatchingDebtBounds.lean:652); `NavierStokes.MatchingConeBounds.exists_ordered_prepared_continuation` (states, NavierStokes/MatchingConeBounds.lean:852); `NavierStokes.TransitionRamp.ordered_seed_bounds` (states, NavierStokes/TransitionRamp.lean:2179); `NavierStokes.OutgoingCone.exists_ordered_profile_cone` (states, NavierStokes/OutgoingCone.lean:503); `NavierStokes.NominalProfile.eventually_matching_geometry` (implies, NavierStokes/NominalProfile.lean:159).
- **(C.16)**, p. 162, proof of Proposition C.2: lean weaker. The paper bounds all η-derivative orders of the stress-vector difference, Σ_{j=0}^m \|∂_η^j(p_{s,N} - p_s)\| ≤ C_m/N, for every m. What I found in Lean is the order-0 statement only: both profile_stocks_rate declarations bound \|profileStockOne (P n) - profileStockOne Q\| and \|profileStockTwo (P n) - profileStockTwo Q\| pointwise on the compact parameter set S, with no η-derivative of the difference bounded. (Lean's two stocks are the two components of the paper's p_s: profileStockOne = X·angularLag/L and profileStockTwo = X·axialLag/(L·E).) The Lean input is one η-derivative deeper than its output — profileData carries the row values together with their first parameterPartial, and profileHistory_first_jet_bound (ModulatedStockBounds.lean:283) feeds it a FiniteJetBound 1 — which matches the paper's own remark that order m of the stocks consumes order m+1 of the histories, at m = 0 only. Severity low rather than medium because I traced the paper's two uses of (C.16) in the proof of Proposition C.2 — preserving the cone inequality between the interval 𝒥 and the first correction interval, and preserving it inside the correction interval — and both are inequalities between stock values at a point, needing only j = 0. I did not find a higher-order η-jet rate for the stocks anywhere in the modulation files; the nearest higher-order statement, ActivationStocks.log_stocks_uniform_jets (:711), is about the activation ramp parameters (T, κ), not about the N-modulation, so it is not a counterpart. Lean: `NavierStokes.ModulatedCone.Repaired.profile_stocks_rate` (states, NavierStokes/ModulatedCone.lean:1248); `NavierStokes.ModulatedStockBounds.profile_stocks_rate` (states, NavierStokes/ModulatedStockBounds.lean:303); `NavierStokes.ModulatedStockBounds.profileData` (uses, NavierStokes/ModulatedStockBounds.lean:26); `NavierStokes.ActivationStocks.profileStockOne` (uses, NavierStokes/ActivationStocks.lean:37); `NavierStokes.ActivationStocks.profileStockTwo` (uses, NavierStokes/ActivationStocks.lean:40).

## Structural notes

From the repository atlas (`work/atlas-result.json`, `arch.notes`):

The "table" on pp. 14-15 is the pair of flowcharts Figures 5 (p13) and 6 (p15).

Structural findings:
1. The paper's intermediate results have no separate Lean statements. No declaration states Theorem 3.1, Proposition 9.9, Proposition 10.1 or Lemmas 10.2/10.3 on their own. They are combined inside GermCandidateAssembly.exists_candidate_witness_of_finite_stages (GermCandidateAssembly.lean:164), whose output is a periodic candidate (ProblemStatement.CandidateProperties, ProblemStatement.lean:101). Theorem 3.1(iii) is restated as a joint-jet limit at (1,0) (VanishingJointJets). Theorem 3.1(ii) is restated as AwayExtensions.
2. The R3 route goes through a periodic step. The paper builds the R3 fields and force directly (Prop 10.1, Lemma 10.3). Lean periodizes and time-activates the cut sums, builds a Borel force for the periodized residual, and then cuts back to R3. The cut-back uses R3CompactCandidate.of_localized_fields, an outer force cutoff (compactForce) and a time cutoff PositiveTimeForce.force; neither cutoff appears in the paper. Corollary 10.6 then periodizes the R3 candidate again, which is the paper's route. A native periodic adapter (option_D_of_candidate) exists but is unused.
3. The blowup is shown differently. Lean uses the axial velocity at the origin, u(t,0) = (1-t)^{-A} j e_z (FinalSlowBase.origin, FinalSlowBase.lean:361). The paper uses the swirl path of Theorem 3.1(iv)/(3.6). Nothing on the main path states (3.6).
4. Lemma 10.4 on the main path is a weaker Gronwall bound. The sharp F(t)^2 inequality is proved (candidate_energy_estimates) but feeds only theorem_1_1_with_dissipation. The maximal-lifespan and H3 claims of p124 exist only for the periodic step (CandidateConsequences.Consequences) and are unused.
5. Steps that exist only in Lean: the comparator adapters, the initial-rest clause |t| <= 3/8 (needed for the compression), and the fixed support cylinder (radius 1/4, height 1/2) in place of the paper's q*-dependent cutoff.
6. The decay exponent sigma_j = 1/5 + j/10 appears only as an abstract gain sequence tending to infinity (StageEstimates.gain).
7. Docstrings cite a different numbering ("Proposition 11.4", "Lemma 11.3", "Proposition 11.7", "Lemma 3.3", "Proposition 5.1"), which does not match this manuscript. Treat docstring cross-references as unreliable.
8. Profile and wave mappings (Section 3.2, Section 3.3) rest only on module docs and were not checked item by item.

Mechanical checks (a comment- and string-aware scan of all 2659 `.lean` files at the pinned commit):

- `NavierStokes/`: 0 `sorry`, 0 `admit`, 0 `axiom` declarations, 0 `native_decide`.
- `sorry` anywhere: 4 occurrences, all in `ComparatorChallenges/Euler.lean`, `ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.lean` (Comparator's challenge placeholders).
- Comparator's permitted axioms (`ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.json`): `propext`, `Quot.sound`, `Classical.choice`.
